# Designed-by-prototype register

Every state/behaviour the Figma mocks do not cover, designed per the module's
own patterns (build plan §0P). Reviewed at each milestone demo.

| # | Milestone | What | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|

| REG-66 | M8 | Review hardening: Approve re-matches each pending row against live leads at approval time (a lead created since the import turns the row into a DUPLICATE instead of a twin; snackbar reports '<n> flagged as duplicates'); a re-imported file is flagged against rows already queued; a duplicate whose target lead was trashed/purged reviews as a plain new lead and the server refuses to merge into a trashed lead; in-file duplicates (no existing lead) show 'Matches: another lead in this file' with Reject/Approve and a 'Duplicate of an earlier row in this file' detail; the NEW radio is offered only when the import has a value, so an empty field can never overwrite a real one; the merge keeps the other phone (and import notes) in Notes to honour '(kept as alt)'; source chips / duplicates toggle can't go stale; the sheet closes itself when the queue empties; bulk bar, snackbar and AI sheet lift above the banner (--bottom-dock) | review fixes; data safety |

| REG-74 | Shell | Ghost loader on every load / refresh (designer rule 2026-08-27): each route ships a `loading.tsx` skeleton mirroring its own layout (`components/shell/skeleton.tsx`, bones on surface-variant with one soft sweep across the sheet, phase pinned to a global clock) and the page is wrapped in `PageReveal`, which holds the skeleton for at least 650 ms after the data arrives, then crossfades (content 360 ms in, ghost 300 ms out). The rail, canvas and snackbar host moved to `app/leads/layout.tsx` so they stay put during the ghost and persist across navigations (expanded rail survives Leads ↔ Import History). In-place refreshes (`router.refresh()`, server-action revalidation) do not replay the ghost. Splitting needs a route segment per tab; the note lives at the top of `app/leads/[id]/[tab]/page.tsx` | perf |
| REG-211 | Cross | Checkbox glyph size (user 2026-08-30): the DS checkbox draws its square edge-to-edge in its 24px box, so it read about a third heavier than the mocks, which use the Material Symbols `check_box_outline_blank` at 24 — an 18px square inside a 24px em box. One rule in `app/globals.css` scoped to `label:has(input[type='checkbox']) svg` brings the drawn glyph to 18px inside the unchanged 24px control, so hit area, alignment and every table / list layout stay as they are (the DS Switch has no svg and is untouched). Also considered and reverted the same day: replacing the grey row hover with a Gmail-style shadow — the user preferred the mocked #f2f2f2 fill after seeing elevation-1 / -2 / -3 and a Y-0 custom lift | user-directed |
| REG-212 | Overview | Two responsive corrections to the Lead Details info column and FILES card (user 2026-08-30), both deviations the mock cannot show because it draws one width. **FILES row**: the mock (858:24943, a 430px card) draws four slots — folder + two files + "+10" — and 858:25922 (396px, quick links expanded) draws the same four shrunk 92 → 83, so a slot is a 92px tile that may shrink to ~83, 8 apart. The card is responsive though, and a fixed three tiles left the row half empty once the info column collapsed. The two mocks are two samples of one behaviour, not two sizes — a 92px tile that shares the row and shrinks as the row tightens. `files-card.tsx` measures the row with a ResizeObserver and picks the slot count whose evenly-shared width lands closest to 92 (`TILE` 92 · `GAP` 8 · `DEFAULT_SLOTS` 4 for the first paint and wherever the width cannot be measured); the CSS then divides the row by `--slots`, so the tiles are equal and **nothing is left over on the right** (the first pass capped them at 92px and left dead space — user 2026-08-30, second round). Measured, trailing space 0 at every width: 1280 → 2 tiles + "+11" at 98 · **1440 → 4 slots at 91.5, the mock** · 1600 → 87.6 · 1920 → 5 tiles + "+8" at 98.3 · 2560 → 9 tiles + "+4" at 87.8, always inside the mocks' own 83–92 band ±. A lead with fewer files than slots keeps the tile size and leaves the row short rather than stretching one file across the card. **Detail values never truncate**: `info-column.module.css` drops `white-space: nowrap` / `text-overflow: ellipsis` from `.text` and `.contactText` for `overflow-wrap: anywhere` (rows keep a 20px min height, `.contactRow` aligns to flex-start), so a long "Property address" wraps to a second line instead of being clipped — this is the detail page, the place a value is read in full. The rule covers every **value** in the column, so the lead name (24/Bold/32) and the assignee names wrap too; their rows grew from a fixed `height` to `min-height` with `align-items: flex-start`, and the name's Edit control keeps a 6px top margin — (32 − 20) / 2 — so at rest the row is pixel-identical to the mock (verified: row 32, pencil 20 at +6). **Labels** (`.key`, `.sectionTitle`) and table / list cells keep their ellipsis: there the column width is the design | user-directed |
| REG-213 | Cross | **Mock-transcribed constants made dynamic** (user 2026-08-30: "a lot of things might be hard code… make them dynamic and just don't decide based on the figma hard coded layout mocks"). A 20-agent audit (10 readers + 10 adversarial skeptics, 69 candidates → 43 confirmed, 26 rejected) found the same defect class the FILES card had, across 37 files. **Rows frozen at one container width:** the Files grid and folder band (`repeat(auto-fill, minmax(299px, 1fr))` — tiles filled only at a 1244px row and orphaned the rest everywhere else); kanban columns (`flex: 1 0 320px; max-width: 480px` — shrink stays 0 so the unscoped 7-column board is untouched); the Questionnaire row (`flex: 1 1 auto` on the questions column, the 480 summary stays fixed); the Qanvast Ops table (`240fr 132fr 138fr 158fr 115fr 369px` — five px tracks dumped all slack into Status, 533px at 1440, and the ghost loader distributed it the other way so every column jumped at hand-off); Settings' panel (25% of the card empty at 1440); the Quotes/Contracts grids (shrinkable floors so the row compresses together instead of being sliced by the body's `overflow: clip`). **Mock FRAME heights transcribed as element heights:** five empty-state cards (`height: 784px` → `flex: 1; min-height: 480px`, the sibling `search-empty.module.css` rule) and both Deleted sheets (`928px` → height to content) — 784 is the output of "the card fills the tab body" at the mock's 24px bottom padding, so `.centre:has([data-testid$='-empty'])` restores that padding for an empty tab and the mock's 784 comes back exactly, without the 64px of pointless scroll. **Counts frozen from sample data:** MY WORK's 2-row cap (its label printed the real total — Farah Ahmad read "Upcoming · 4" above 2 rows), kanban card avatars (measured like the FILES row), the import wizard's step-3 preview (rendered all 14 mock columns instead of the 7 mapped: 1,149px of empty grid with a populated column stranded past two blank ones), the leads PDF export's character cap (now derived from the real column pitch). **Truncation on values that must be read:** the Activity feed sentence and the file viewer's Information panel now wrap (REG-212's rule). **Menus positioned by row count → by measurement:** the Quotes / Contracts / listing row kebabs decide their side from the space actually below the trigger; the shared menu caps its height and scrolls; the Customise popover is viewport-aware. **Sample data shown as real:** the Won panel offered `Contract_OliviaM_signed.pdf` on all 36 leads (and persisted it) — now derived from the lead, still exactly that string for Olivia; the Task panel's attachment tiles show the real file's thumbnail and kind instead of the Move-stage chat screenshot + PDF glyph; a picked `.pdf` renders as a PDF in the Return wizard and the attach modal; a lead's second contract is numbered. Rejected as design intent, not defects: 26, including a proposed ResizeObserver on a column that is a fixed 191px on every viewport | audit 2026-08-30 / user-directed |
| REG-214 | Settings/Quotes | **Quote approval is a studio setting and the quote's lifecycle derives from it** (user 2026-08-30: "in this studio setup the quote required approval… now it has to be dynamic based on the studio setup"). New `quote_approval` key in `settings` (`lib/settings/types.ts`, default `required` — the studio the mocks were drawn for), a second Settings › Leads section built on the Stage movement pattern, and `quoteStatuses(approval)` / `quoteStepIndex()` in `lib/leads/quote-filters.ts`: **required** = Draft → Sent for Approval → Approved → Sent to Client → Signed; **not required** = Draft → Sent to Client → Signed. One derivation feeds all three surfaces — the Overview widget's track, the row status menu and the Filters modal — so a studio can never set a status its own widget does not show. The widget's track was the mock's fixed "Draft · Sent · Approved" (858:25057); it is now one bar per stage, each **as wide as the label beneath it and starting where that label starts** (a 2-row CSS grid over `max-content` columns, `justify-content: space-between`), labels on one line, never wrapped or broken. Iterated with the user: equal segments → wrapped "Sent for Approval"; single-line spread → labels not aligned to the notches; text-width segments → accepted. A quote holding a status the studio has since retired still reads at the furthest step it actually passed | user-directed |
| REG-215 | Tasks | Tasks tab round (user 2026-08-30, seven items): Create quick links › **Task** now behaves like Quote / Note / Question — in place on its own tab, otherwise `?new=1` on `/tasks` (it called `openTasks(myWorkRef.current)`, an anchor that only exists on the Overview, so from every other tab it did nothing at all); **Add Task docks** to the viewport bottom while the table runs past the fold (`position: sticky` + a 1px sentinel driving the level-1 lift, the house dock pattern) and settles at the card's end; the completed **tick is the filled** `check_circle`; the **comment count is its own control** — it opens the Comments panel directly rather than the task detail, so the chips moved out of the title button (buttons cannot nest, the reason the status glyph already sat outside) with a filler taking the cell's slack, and the chip carries a full button reset so it is the attachment chip exactly — no border, no elevation, nothing the DS does not draw; **Shift-click selects the run** between the last row touched and this one, adding only (a mail client's behaviour), the modifier read off the click in capture because the DS Checkbox reports the next checked state and not the event; a **new task lands unseen** (`seenAt: null`) so it is the first row whatever the sort and wears the mock's RW-New band + bold title (902:26362 frame 191) — it was stamped seen on creation, which put it at the BOTTOM of the default due-date sort; the **tag picker's field is a column** — the input keeps the top line and picked tags flow beneath, so the field grows downwards instead of squeezing the input further right with every tag | user-directed |
| REG-216 | Cross | Three smaller corrections (user 2026-08-30): the DS **SearchBar's doubled focus ring** — the bar already draws the mocks' 2px primary inside stroke and the global `:focus-visible` added a second 2px outline outside it, so every tab's search focused as a double rectangle while the listing's hand-rolled search focused as one; the input now drops the global ring (`[class*='searchBar'] input:focus-visible`). The card's **↗ open control** gains the hover plate every other icon control has (24px control, negative margin, glyph still the mock's 16 in the mock's place). The **source chip is green only for Qanvast** — the source that actually behaves differently (Return Lead, Qanvast Ops); every other source is a plain chip, so the colour carries meaning | user-directed |
| REG-217 | Shell | **Tab navigation no longer flashes its ghost** (user 2026-08-30: "every time i click a tab it loads", "why is there a jerk"). Three causes, all fixed: `PageReveal` held the skeleton for a **minimum 650ms after the page arrived**, and when the server answered before `loading.tsx` ever painted it still waited the full beat — so a fast navigation got MORE skeleton than a slow one; the hold now applies only when a ghost actually painted (`ghostClock.shownAt` non-null), which keeps the full beat on cold loads and removes it entirely on warm ones. The tab links prefetch in **full** (`prefetch` — the default fetches only the loading boundary, i.e. exactly the flash being removed), and `experimental.staleTimes.dynamic = 30` stops the router treating every `force-dynamic` entry as stale on arrival, which was re-fetching what had just been prefetched. Measured at 1440 across all seven tabs: ghost visible 373ms / 452ms on Quotes / Notes before, **0ms on every tab** after. Safe because every mutation goes through a server action that calls `revalidatePath`, which evicts those entries | user-directed |
| REG-218 | Questionnaire | Unanswered questions sort to the top of the tab; answered ones follow as a record (user 2026-08-30). A stable sort in `fetchLeadQuestions`, so each group keeps the tab's own `sortOrder` / `createdAt` sequence | user-directed |
| REG-219 | Cross | **State frozen from the mock made dynamic** (user 2026-08-30: "since we designed in Figma it will be hard coded and once we build the prototype no hardcoded things should exists"). REG-213 swept LAYOUT hard-coding; this is the other class — a mock is a picture of ONE moment (one selected item, one route, one status), and building it faithfully bakes that moment in as a constant. An 18-agent audit confirmed 23 of 39 candidates. The shape, and the case that named it: `app/leads/app-rail.tsx` listed `{ icon: 'people_alt', label: 'Leads', href: '/leads', fill: true }` — the mock was drawn on the Leads screen, so the filled glyph became a property of the ITEM and Leads read as selected on Qanvast Ops and Settings too; now `fill={active}`, a value the component already computed. **Wrong data reaching the DB:** `uploadFile` wrote the mock's single row (kind image · the floor-plan thumbnail · image/jpeg) whatever was picked, so a PDF showed correctly right up to the moment it was saved and then became a photograph — it now derives kind / mime / thumb from the name, exactly as the two client surfaces already did; the Files tab dropped the picked name entirely, persisting every upload as the action's `'Living Room'` default; `addTask` omitted `creatorId` where every other writer stamps it, so tasks added from the listing / Overview popovers and from Suggested tasks read "System created the task" and vanished from the Creators filter; `setQuoteStatus` back-filled `approvedAt` on **Signed** as well as Approved, wrong in a studio with no approval step (REG-214); the import parser accepted only the five active `STAGES` where the vocabulary is `ALL_STAGES`, silently landing an imported On Hold / Lost row in New. **A gate enforced in one place and granted in another:** Create quick links › Call Log / Meeting set the Move-stage target directly, past the page's own `requestStageChange` — on Olivia (New, linear) the header pill refuses Met with "Complete Contacted first" while the quick link opened it, and `moveStage` has no server guard, so the lead landed in Met with no Contacted log. Gated via a new `openStageLog()` that opens the window straight only for a current or completed stage (routing everything through `requestStageChange` would have made Call Log a no-op on a lead already in Contacted and lost REG-168's "add another log"). **Capabilities frozen at the mock moment:** the FILES card's tiles answered "coming soon" behind the comment "no tab designed yet" — untrue since REG-181, and the card's own "+n" tile already navigated; they now open the folder (`?folder=`) or the viewer. **A cold load showing another screen's ghost:** `[id]/loading.tsx` rendered the Overview skeleton for everything beneath it, so a hard load of a wide tab painted an info-column layout that route does not have and then handed over to a differently shaped one — the failure REG-74 exists to prevent. A new `[tab]/loading.tsx` derives the variant from the pathname (guarding `meetings`, which really is narrow); warm tab clicks are untouched (REG-217). **Bulk delete overreaching:** "Delete All" appeared when the selection covered the VISIBLE rows, so filtering to one match and pressing select-all offered to delete every task on the lead; it now compares against the lead's full list, as the toolbar's own Delete all does. Also: the Save-as-Template switches reset to true instead of reading the chosen template's stored values; the attach toast painted the mock photo beside a picked PDF; the merge preview computed "Merged result" differently from the action that merges (promising a value for three fields the merge does not write); the Quotes table always drew "Approval Date" though the component knows the studio's lifecycle; the XLSX writer named every worksheet "Leads"; the Contacted brief asserted "no questionnaire yet" as a constant beside a card counting 7 / 11 answered; the wizard dated its auto-added task from the FIRST log with a next step rather than the newest; `validateStage` required the next-step fields for one hard-coded outcome where the panel decides from `NEXT_STEP_BY_OUTCOME`; two file glyphs were `'image'` outright, reachable only once uploads could persist a PDF | audit 2026-08-30 / user-directed |
| REG-220 | Files | `attachedTo` reported `'Task'` or nothing although three relations point at `lead_files`, so a file attached only to a note or a return read as unattached. It now derives from all three (`task_attachments` · `note_attachments` · `return_attachments`) with one tag per row, Task winning ties. **New copy**: "Note" and "Return" as Attached-to words — the mock (931:40181) repeats "Task" on every row as a placeholder, so neither appears in it. Demo-visible in list view only (the grid has no such column): one of Olivia's Q-101 images moves from "--" to "Note", and eight files across five other leads move to "Return" — the very rows the return details modal already lists | audit 2026-08-30 / invented copy |
| REG-221 | Overview | Two feed wordings corrected against the app's own derivation: the Overview RECENT ACTIVITY card said "Updated contact log" for any Contacted wizard log whatever stage it came FROM (Cheryl Lim, Priya Nair, Kai Teo each carry a New→Contacted log and now read "Moved to Contacted", agreeing with the Activity tab), and the Activity tab said "You marked the lead as lost" for a Qanvast return whose stage log carries the real reason in its payload. **Not done, deliberately**: feeding the card from `fetchLeadActivity` (the app's real derivation of the same feed). It cannot preserve the mocked card — Olivia's four rows would be replaced by her task, note and eleven question rows with different wording — and `fetchLeadDetail` is the cached path `generateMetadata` and the page share, so folding ~12 more queries into it costs every lead route. The card keeps its own narrower derivation; the two wordings now match | audit 2026-08-30 |
| REG-222 | Cross | The completed **green check is filled everywhere it appears**: the LEAD STAGES card's done rows drew `check_circle` outlined while the Move stage rail and the task tick already drew it filled (user 2026-08-31). One glyph, one state, three surfaces | user-directed |
| REG-223 | Cross | **Remembered UI state is one mechanism** (user 2026-08-31: "when i collapse a section remember the user preference … not just in this, across the prototype … we had made for the leads list vs kanban view too"). `lib/prefs.ts` generalises what `lib/leads/view-pref.ts` (table vs kanban) and `quick-links.tsx` already did: a localStorage flag read through `useSyncExternalStore`, whose **server snapshot is always the caller's default** so React hydrates against the mock's own state and the stored value takes over on the first client render. Wired to the Overview info column's four sections (`om.detail.section.<title>`) and MY WORK › Upcoming (`om.detail.myWork.upcoming`). Per browser, not per lead — a section closed because the user does not want to read it stays closed on the next lead. Maison AI › "Reset prototype to base" already clears every `om.*` key, so these ride along | user-directed |
| REG-224 | Cross | **A surface opened to enter data opens with the caret in its field** (user 2026-08-31: "the user shouldn't have to look for the field and then click to enter the details"). Most already did — the name / move / manage-tags / template dialogs, the add-question card, the comment composers, the tag picker, the inline task row, the Move stage panels. The two that did not now do: the Notes editor (title) and the contact-log editor (Outcome) | user-directed |
| REG-225 | Overview | LEAD STAGES checked against 858:25211 (Lost lead) — three corrections, two user-directed (2026-08-31). **Durations were missing**: every completed row read "--" where the mock reads "2 days". Not a card bug — every seeded parked lead carried exactly ONE stage log, the one that parked it (`Contacted → Lost`), so nothing recorded when the lead ENTERED Contacted and the card had no span to measure. `scripts/backfill-stage-logs.ts` (idempotent) adds the missing `New → <parked-from>` transition to each, placed between creation and parking so both stages get a real whole-day span (two days in New where there is room, otherwise half), with payload `{"quick":true}` — the move happened, no details captured — rather than an invented contact log. Baseline re-snapshotted, 209 → 214 rows; David Koh now reads New 2 days · Contacted 6 days. **Lost glyph filled** (`cancel`), as every other status glyph on the card is since REG-222 — the mock draws both it and the green check at 20/Light, so filled is the user's standing override, not the mock. **A Lost lead's un-reached stages are refused**: the rule lives in `blockedReason` ("Resume at <stage> first"), so the stages card, the header pill, the listing dropdown and kanban drops all inherit it; the stages it DID reach stay open, which is the way back. On Hold is untouched — a pause is not an end. The mock draws those rows enabled, so this is user-directed (asked as "once the lead is lost then why we need to enable the not completed milestones"). Note the parked gate is consulted only on the parked path: routing every `requestStageChange` through `blockedReason` would also have started enforcing REG-130's "Assign a member first" on the Overview quick links, changing the hero lead's demo flow, which nobody asked for | user-directed / mock |
| REG-226 | Cross | **A form opened from one field opens ON that field** (user 2026-08-31: "i clicked only on one field of add property address, go to that field and make it active … it is hard for me to go through the entire fields list"). The Lead form's `form` state gained an optional `focus`, so pressing "Add" beside Property address opens the modal, scrolls that row into view and puts the caret in it — instead of dropping the user at the top of fifteen fields. Generic by construction: every field carries `data-field`, `IN_MORE` names the ones behind "Additional info" so the section expands first, and the two-frame wait lets the modal's entrance and that expansion finish. **Create New Lead focuses its first field**; a plain **Edit** still opens unfocused, because nothing was singled out. `LeadFormOpen` is now one exported type shared by the listing and the Lead Details page. REG-224 recorded this rule but only reached the note editors — the case the user actually meant, the Lead form, was missed | user-directed |
| REG-227 | Overview | **"Notes" renamed to Remarks** on the lead — the info column row and the Lead form field (label, placeholder, aria-label) — because "Notes" collided with the Notes tab, which is a different record entirely (`lead_notes`, not `leads.notes`). The tab keeps its name; the data key stays `notes`. The field's helper claimed "You can view these notes under the Notes page in Leads Detail" — it never appeared there, so it now reads "Shown under Other info on the lead's Overview" (corrected copy, flagged) | user-directed / invented copy |
| REG-228 | Overview | **Per-row "Add" links dropped from the info column**: an empty Property address reads "--" like Area size and Key collection, since the header's Edit opens the whole form and a user fills everything in one pass rather than one field at a time (user 2026-08-31). The dead `onAddAddress` / `onAddNotes` props left `InfoColumn` and the page. REG-226's field-targeting mechanism stays — `focus` on the form's open state, `data-field` on every field, `IN_MORE` for the collapsed section — because the page checklist mandates it for any future per-field entry point and because "Create New Lead focuses its first field" runs through the same code. It simply has no caller on the edit path any more | user-directed |

Swapped in the JSX rather than with `row-reverse`, so DOM order still matches screen order and the tab sequence follows what the eye sees | user-directed |
| REG-230 | Shell | **The tabs dock with the header.** The L2 header is sticky at 8 and 72 tall, so the tab row sticks at 80 (`overview.module.css .stickyTabs`, passed to the shared `Tabs` as a className so no other host changes), z 19 below the header's 20 so the header always covers it and never the reverse. It already carried the white plate and level-1 shadow that separate it from rows passing underneath. Verified at a 1200px scroll: header top 8, tabs top 80, gap 0. Qanvast Ops' tab row is unchanged — its L1 header has no fixed height, so it needs its own offset if wanted | user-directed |
| REG-231 | Leads | **Assign member field in the Lead form** (user 2026-08-31: "after property address, please add assign member input field which search and dropdown of the members that we have today"). `components/leads/assignee-field.tsx` — a searchable, **multi-select** member picker sitting directly after Property address, in both create and edit. Multi-select is not a preference: `lead_assignees` is many-to-many and `LeadRow.assignees` is a `Member[]`, so a single-select would silently drop the second owner of any lead opened and saved here. **No mock draws this field**, so nothing is invented from nothing — the box is `components/om/text-field.tsx`, the dropdown is `components/om/portal-menu.tsx` (the only anchored surface that clears the modal's z 100 and is not clipped by its scrolling body — the Assign popover's z 70 and `dropdown.tsx`'s absolute z 60 both fail there), the rows are the Assign popover's own vocabulary (Avatar 40 · name · role with "(you)"), and the picked members read as the tag picker's outlined chips. **Invented and logged**: the field itself and with it a second assignment door; search over members (nothing in the product filters a member list today — the Assign popover renders `team.map` unfiltered and its mock 780:87942 has no search); the chips presentation; and the copy — label "Assign member", placeholder "Search members", empty line "No members match “…”". **Persistence**: `assignees: string[]` joins `LeadFormData` AND `LeadFormInput` (two independent declarations — adding it to only one compiles clean and silently drops the value), and `createLead` / `updateLead` now write the lead and its join rows in ONE `db.transaction` via a shared `writeAssignees`, which also de-duplicates against `unique(leadId, memberId)`; `assignMembers` was refactored onto the same writer and stopped being non-atomic. Neither host's `onSubmit` changed. **REG-130 upheld, and this supersedes its closing clause "Assignment stays in the listing's Assign popover"**: `validateLeadForm(data, requireAssignee)` refuses an empty selection when editing a lead past New, the same gate the popover's `requireOne` applies — otherwise the form would be a second way to clear the last owner off a Contacted lead. A lead created with an owner now clears `blockedReason`'s New gate without a second trip to the popover | user-directed / invented copy |
| REG-232 | Shell | **The rail remembers being pinned open** (user 2026-08-31: "when i click and go to some other page the sidebar collapses which should not be"). It held plain `useState(false)`, and `AppShell` is mounted by each route group's own layout — so crossing from Leads to Settings or Qanvast Ops remounted the rail and dropped the choice. Now `useFlagPref('om.rail.expanded')` (lib/prefs.ts, REG-223's mechanism). `useFlagPref` gained a third return, `touched`: true only once the viewer has toggled the flag this session. The rail's width transition is gated on it (`[data-animate]`), so a remembered rail arrives already open instead of replaying its expand on every page load — the quick-links strip's rule, now shared | user-directed |
| REG-233 | Leads | Picked members sit **inside** the Assign member box, under the search row, not in a strip beneath it (user 2026-08-31) — the shape the tag picker already uses. `components/om/text-field.tsx` gained an optional `below` slot rendered inside the box: the box then grows from its fixed 48px into a column, and the label floats by definition (a box holding content is never empty). The control row only gets a wrapper element when `below` is passed, and every style is scoped to `[data-below]`, so the ~30 other fields keep byte-identical markup. Verified: empty box 48px, one member picked 76px with the chip inside it and the label on the border | user-directed |
| REG-234 | Overview | The source tag carries a leading glyph, `share` (user 2026-08-31). A deviation: the mock draws this one chip WITHOUT an icon — "Tag Outlined Green (no icon)", 01-header spec — while its three neighbours (call · money_bag · home) have one. **One glyph for every source, not a per-source map**: Material Symbols ships no brand marks, so Referral and Walk-in could be honest while Instagram, Facebook, Qanvast, Google Ads and Meta Ads would be invented; real brand marks need custom SVG assets. The glyph is **`database`** (owner's pick, 2026-08-31; `share` was the first attempt). It was not in the subsetted font, so both faces were regenerated per the CLAUDE.md recipe — 151 names now, resolved through the GSUB ligature table with their `.fill` variants, rounded 88 → 90 KB and outlined 34 KB, `public/fonts/ICONS.txt` updated. Verified after: 259 glyphs across three routes, none rendering as the literal word, and still no external font request | user-directed |

A lead that HAS tasks still opens on its list: there the intent is to see them. The composer is focused whenever it appears, in both paths (REG-224's rule) | user-directed |

Present since REG-1's phase-1 commit and only surfaced now, on a fresh machine where the build was run from a clean checkout. Nothing was lost: no file imports `Table` from `@/components/ds` — every table in the product is hand-built (`components/leads/table.tsx`, `tasks-table.tsx`, `quotes-table.tsx`, `contracts-table.tsx`). The other 70 importers of `@/components/ds` are untouched | bug fix |
| REG-237 | Overview | **The Maison AI brief carousel is now a stack behind Show more** (user 2026-09-01: the widget carries must-see AI suggestions, and a carousel hides them behind blind paging — "please add two cards below and add a show more button"). The mock's pager row (858:24867: dots + ‹ › Icon Buttons 24) is replaced by a 24px toggle row holding a "Show more" expander in the lead form's "Additional info" voice (subheading-m + secondary chevron, expand_more ↔ keyboard_arrow_up); pressing it stacks the stage's remaining brief cards (unchanged card design, 8 gap) between the first card and the row, all visible at once, and flips to "Show less". At rest the block is the first (primary) brief + the row — the mock block's exact 208px, so the fold and the ghost skeleton hold. A stage change re-collapses to the new stage's primary brief; a stage with a single brief (defensive) hides the row. "Show more" / "Show less" is invented copy | user-directed |
| REG-238 | Shell | **Warm tab hops are a single atomic paint** (user 2026-09-01: "there is a jerk … I wanted no jerk and smooth navigation with zero load"). REG-217's warm-hop exemption never actually fired: it read `ghostClock.shownAt` in an effect, but PageReveal's OWN skeleton is a `GhostSheet`, which stamps that clock in a layout effect — and child effects run before the parent's — so every remount read its own stamp, concluded a loading ghost had painted, and ran the full cold treatment (650ms hold + two crossfades, ~900ms of skeleton measured per hop) over content the router cache had already answered with zero network. PageReveal now decides cold-vs-instant during render (lazy initialiser), where a stamp can only have come from a loading.tsx ghost painted in an earlier commit: warm hop with a cache hit = first paint is the real page, no ghost, no fade (measured: 0 ghost frames, content opacity never below 1.00, all hops). Two edges the fix had to keep: the initial load's ghost is the server-streamed fallback, swapped away before any client GhostSheet mounts and so never stamped — a module-level `boot` flag marks that load cold, anchored to first-contentful-paint (measured after: ghost from frame 1, ~650ms beat + fade, both skeleton variants); a warm hop that genuinely fetches still stamps via loading.tsx and keeps the honest hold. Also `staleTimes` 30s → 300s (dynamic and static): a tab explained for a minute mid-pitch went stale and the next hop refetched; the safety argument is REG-217's own — every mutation revalidates the path | bug fix |

Placed after Assignees (the people columns together), 160px like Assignees, header sortable-inert like its neighbours; the inline add row shows "—" until save stamps the creator (REG-219). The five format buttons read `document.queryCommandState` — execCommand's reader twin — on every `selectionchange` and right after each command, committing state only when a bit flips so caret movement doesn't re-render; a pressed button wears the icon-button selected lavender (om/icon-button.module.css `data-selected`) via `aria-pressed`, which also announces the toggle to screen readers. States are ignored while formatting is disabled (a contact log before Edit). Verified: plain=false → Bold=true (lavender) → reselect=true → unbold=false | user-directed |

The app-wide reset (globals.css `ul, ol { list-style: none }`) — written when no screen rendered a content list — stripped the markers off the editor's lists; `.rich ul { list-style: disc }` / `.rich ol { decimal }` restore them, read mode included (same class). Verified: disc + decimal computed on toolbar-made lists, 24px indent | bug fix |

The query flips to created desc (the mock's frames 162/165 drew creation order — the note above the newer log); the in-progress draft pins to the TOP as the newest thing of all, and the default selection follows the top item, so the tab opens on the latest record. | REG-269 | Cross | **Responsive conversion — mobile + tablet** (user 2026-09-01: "convert all our screens to mobile and tablet responsive"). No mobile mocks exist, so the system is designer-directed and written down first: `design-source/context/responsive.md` (breakpoints mobile ≤767 · tablet 768–1199 · desktop ≥1200 = the mocked design, UNCHANGED; per-pattern rules; ground rules). An 11-agent audit measured every surface's hazards first (per-surface briefs in the session scratchpad). **Shell foundation:** new `components/shell/mobile-nav.tsx` — a 56px top bar whose hamburger slides the SAME rail in as a 216px off-canvas drawer (`AppRail` gained `drawer` / `onNavigate`; the drawer copy must NOT write `--rail-w`, or overlays would follow a rail that is not there); the rail hides ≤767 and is locked collapsed 768–1199 (a pinned 216 starves content, and the remembered pref resumes ≥1200); page sheet goes edge-to-edge. **Shared primitives:** dialogs clamp to the viewport ≤1199 and the form/modal sizes become full-screen sheets ≤767; drawers `max-width: 100vw`; the snackbar spans the viewport instead of anchoring to `--rail-w` + a locked 344px bar; L1/L2 headers wrap (the L2 leaves the sticky stack ≤767 — 124px of chrome is too much of an 844px screen — and its chips take their own scrollable line); the tab row hides its scrollbar and centres the active tab by setting **its own scrollLeft** (`scrollIntoView` also scrolls ancestors, which displaced the sticky strip); ghost skeletons unclip (the desktop track width moved from an inline style, which no media query could override, to `data-min`). **`body { overflow-x: hidden }` removed** — it silently clipped every overflow, hiding exactly the failures this work fixes; the stale unused `--app-*` constants went with it. **Surfaces:** tables (tasks · quotes · contracts · files list) scroll inside their own card ≤1199 — the audit found content measured up to 2.5× its container with `overflow-x: visible`, so columns were unreachable behind the sheet's `overflow: clip`; toolbars wrap with a full-width search ≤767; the detail frame stacks (info column full-width above the centre, quick-links strip hidden, card grid single-column ≤1199); Notes becomes a master-detail push with a back affordance; the Activity row puts date-time above the sentence; Questionnaire stacks its summary; the Files grid goes fluid (auto-fill minmax 160/220). **Verified:** a route × viewport gate (`verify-responsive.cjs`, 11 routes × 390/768/1440) passes with zero page-level horizontal overflow, and every desktop route is BYTE-IDENTICAL to its pre-conversion baseline. That byte-compare caught a real regression: appending to `tabs.module.css` flipped the cascade so `.tabs { position: relative }` beat `.stickyTabs { position: sticky }`, leaving the row with `top: 80px` as a relative offset — the strip painted 80px low on every detail tab. Fixed by doubling the class (`.stickyTabs.stickyTabs`) so order can never decide it | user-directed |

| REG-273 | Cross | **Mobile and tablet are a touch app now** (user 2026-09-01, clarifying REG-269: "mirror desktop as-is meaning the design language and features are from the desktop goes to mobile and tablet"). The earlier reading froze desktop pixel sizes everywhere; the owner meant the design LANGUAGE and every FEATURE carry over, and a phone gets touch ergonomics. Policy written into responsive.md and the page checklist: **every control has a ≥40px box on a touch pointer**, the box grows and the drawn mark keeps its mocked size, the extra given back as negative margin so no row reflows; **nothing is hover-only**; anything explained only by a Tooltip gets a second channel, because tooltips never fire for touch. Six surface agents grew the boxes surface by surface (table sort buttons, row kebabs and status glyphs, count chips, due/tag buttons, stage pills, kanban card controls, the Notes pane's six format buttons, file tiles, Settings rows, Qanvast actions, the info column's copy/WhatsApp glyphs), and the shared families were lifted centrally in globals.css / icon-button.module.css / menus.module.css / app-rail.module.css so every surface moves together: om icon buttons (24 and 36), DS checkboxes, DS links, DS `md` buttons and SearchBars, segmented controls, menu rows, rail items. **Two interaction fixes, not just sizing:** the Files grid's desktop double-tap-to-open is hostile on a phone, so ≤767 a single tap OPENS and selection moves to an explicit checkbox (the affordance the list view already has, hidden above 767 so the mock is untouched); and a locked stage in the stage dropdown — whose reason lived only in a Tooltip — now answers a tap with the same sentence in the app's snackbar, gated on `matchMedia('(hover: none)')` read in the handler so desktop is unchanged. **Keyed to the pointer, not the width:** several surfaces had scoped their growth to `max-width: 767px`, which a 10-inch touch tablet never matches, so the shared families use `@media (hover: none)` — a mouse always reports `hover: hover`, which is also why desktop cannot see any of it. **Verified** by a new touch gate that runs 390 and 768 as real touch devices (`hasTouch`) — the previous gate ran a mouse browser and never exercised a single one of these rules, a hole in my own verification worth recording. It fails on any interactive control under 40px or left invisible by a hover-only rule: from 25 flagged route/viewport combos down to 10, and every remaining flag is the same exemption — an inline link inside a prose sentence (the Activity timeline's row text), which WCAG 2.2's target-size rule exempts for exactly the reason growing it would break the line rhythm. | REG-285 | Cross | **Production-readiness pass: the four integrity fixes** (user 2026-09-01: "make sure everything is product ready code, since my team later will be integrating this with their backend"). From a nine-dimension audit whose 71 findings were each put to an adversarial verifier — 38 stood, 33 were refuted. Fixed here, the ones that are wrong CODE rather than prototype scope: (a) **`updateTask` spread a caller-supplied patch into `db.update().set()`** — Drizzle writes any key matching a column, so `leadId`, `creatorId`, `seenAt` and `createdAt` were all writable from the wire and a request could move a task to another lead; it now copies field by field with runtime type checks, and `kind` validates against a new `TASK_KINDS` in lib/leads/enums.ts (the vocabulary previously existed only as a schema comment). (b) **The Notes autosave could create the same note repeatedly** — `savingId` clears the instant `createNote` resolves while the draft is still mounted and dirty, so if the revalidated data does not yet carry the row (read replica, split read/write services, a dropped revalidation — all normal against a real backend) the 900ms debounce fired `createNote` again, unbounded; the create is now guarded on both sides by `savedDraftId`. (c) **A missing `DATABASE_URL` silently fell back to `file:./local.db`** — in a container that is an empty database, on a dev machine it is the DEMO data, and both look like a working app serving the wrong records; production now fails at boot with a message naming the fix, and a remote URL without an auth token does too. The guard is skipped during `next build` (NEXT_PHASE), so a CI build without runtime config still succeeds. Verified across all four env combinations. (d) The register's own dead code: `restoreLead` (superseded by the bulk `restoreLeads`), three unreferenced CSS classes, and a note marking `createQuestion` / `deleteQuestion` as the deliberately-reserved studio-level seam rather than leftovers | user-directed |
| REG-286 | Cross | **The production-readiness audit's 34 remaining findings, fixed** (user 2026-09-01: "fix everything", then "don't leave anything, fix everything once for all"). 50 fixes across eight areas. **Demo-visible:** the Notes autosave no longer loses text — the 900ms debounce is now flushed when the user leaves the item and when the tab unmounts, one save in flight per item, and the draft→saved handover keeps the editor mounted (it used to remount, jumping focus to Title and discarding keystrokes typed during the create); "which way does a row kebab open" became ONE helper instead of five copies, so Tasks and Files stopped throwing their last menus up over the toolbar; the stage-movement dispatch is shared, so a failed Flexible-mode move from the detail page reports instead of raising an unhandled rejection; ONE phone rule and ONE strict date parser replaced four and three copies (the import path accepted 32/13/2026; the lead form's field did `digitsOnly().slice(0,8)`, so a pasted "+65 9123 4567" stored "65912345"). **Backend seams:** transactions around every multi-write mutation, runtime validation on the enum-shaped write paths, one home for the `ME = 'dane'` session-user stand-in, one revalidation helper covering every detail tab (Activity had none while `staleTimes` held entries for 300s), a by-id detail loader built on a SHARED row-builder so the listing and detail paths cannot drift, `server-only` on the loaders, and the dead `lead_questionnaires` type retired. **A11y:** `main` landmark, skip link, `role="cell"` on four grids, and the focus/Escape policy adopted by every hand-rolled modal. **Refuted, deliberately not "fixed":** `.filterBadge` had no drift, `app/settings/actions.ts` already validated, the rail already had accessible names, and the "Contracts is a verbatim fork of Quotes" finding was measurably wrong — the stylesheets differ by 18–134 lines and contracts-table differs on 136 of 200 lines because the tables carry different columns; collapsing them would have replaced two readable files with a worse abstraction. Verified: 13-case behaviour gate (every case fails on pre-fix code), search 15/15, responsive gate clean at 390/768/1440, desktop byte-identical | user-directed |
| REG-287 | Cross | **The focus trap was silently off wherever it mattered most** — found by testing, not by review, while closing REG-286's gaps. Two real bugs in the shared `components/om/surface.ts` policy: (1) `isTopmostSurface` skipped a surface's ANCESTORS but not its DESCENDANTS, and `compareDocumentPosition` reports a descendant as FOLLOWING — so any nested surface demoted its own host; (2) worse, the Tab trap deferred to `isTopmostSurface` at all. The Lead form's assignee picker portals a `role="listbox"` to `<body>` that stays mounted while focus tabs onward, so from the moment that field was reached the form switched OFF its own trap and Tab walked into the listing behind an `aria-modal` dialog. Esc still uses the topmost rule (the top layer owns Esc); the trap now asks only where focus IS — the handler's existing "focus inside another surface keeps that surface's tab order" check is the precise version. The trap also moved to the CAPTURE phase, because the DS Button is a react-aria pressable that stops keydown propagation, so a bubble listener never saw the Tab leaving the last control. Also fixed: `lead-form.tsx` never received the trap at all (my own planning error — it was moved between agent allowlists for the phone fix and lost the assignment). Verified: trapped through 25 Tabs and 15 Shift+Tabs, Escape closes, and the four hand-rolled modals in the gate still trap | bug fix |
| REG-288 | Questionnaire | Ask the client dialog: the primary button reads **"Send via WhatsApp"** (user 2026-09-02), not the mock's "Send Notification" (931:46853 / spec 01-listing-embed) — the studio reaches the client on WhatsApp, so the button names the channel it actually uses. Behaviour is unchanged: it still closes the dialog and answers "Notification sent to <lead>" (REG-188). The mock files keep the old label as the record of what was drawn. | user-directed |
| REG-289 | Overview | Create quick links (858:26317 / 858:25094): **Note** now sits above **Contact Log** (user 2026-09-02) — the mock orders them Contact Log then Note. The rich "Write a note..." body is replaced by **Remarks**: the Move stage window's own label + `NotesArea`, sitting with the other fields; in view mode it is the last label/value row, wrapping, and it is omitted when empty. The rule that separated fields from body is gone with the body. A log saved while the body was rich carries HTML, so `draftFrom` flattens a contact body through `bodyText` on the way into the draft — each log converts once, the first time it is opened. Auto Fill is now the Move stage window's own `LinkButton` carrying the **FAB's Maison AI sparkle** (`brief-sparkle.svg`), and the stage window's Auto Fill moves to that mark too — `sparkle.svg`, the mock's asset there, is a different four-pointed shape, and Auto Fill is one Maison AI action wherever it appears. **Consequence to note:** the bar was also the contact log's only Attach file entry point, so a log's attachments can now be viewed but not added from this tab. | user-directed |
| REG-292 | Notes | **Every item opens under a header naming its kind** (user 2026-09-02): "Note" or "Contact log", in a container with the mock toolbar's own 68px / p16/24 / bottom-rule treatment (931:44905) — the kind on the left, the item's actions on the right (a note's are the format tools; a contact log's are Auto Fill while editing, Edit otherwise, or the REG-268 lock hint). One strip, never two: the contact log's "Contact log" block title and its inline REG-256 Edit moved OUT of the editor into this header, because a header has to be a child of the pane for its rule to span the pane and for the fields to scroll under it. The empty pane is unchanged — with no title, the tools sit left exactly where the bare toolbar drew them. | user-directed |
| REG-293 | Notes | **The Notes tab gets its own toolbar row** (user 2026-09-02). The tab had no page-level row: the kind chips (931:44884), New ▾ and the REG-275 search sat inside the list column, so Notes started 60px higher than every sibling and had no heading of its own. Built on the lead listing's toolbar (`components/leads/toolbar.tsx`), the precedent the owner named — its "All Leads (24) ▾" pill is a scope filter standing where a title would, with the search beside it — because Notes has exactly that shape (All / Notes / Contact Log over one list). The chips become an **"All Notes & Logs (n) ▾" scope pill** (label per REG-296) whose menu items are the StageFilterDropdown's label + count rows (counts = every item the list can hold before the scope and the query, the draft included), New ▾ keeps the siblings' position as the first control on the right (as Add Task / New Quote) with the search beside it at the row's end. The list column is only the items now, scrolling inside it, and the card fills what the toolbar leaves instead of standing at the mock's 844. | REG-294 | Notes | **Searching never steals the caret** (user 2026-09-02: "when i start typing within search bar and suddenly it moves to note editor automatically"). The editor is keyed on the selected item, so a new selection remounts it and its first field's autoFocus fires. That is right when the user chose the item (a row click, New ▾) and wrong when the selection moved by itself: a query narrows the list, the selected row drops out, the newest-first fallback (REG-262) picks another, and the caret left the search box mid-word. Intent decides now — `lead-notes.tsx` keeps a `mayFocusEditor` flag that a row click and New ▾ set and the fallback clears, and both editors take it as their `autoFocus`. Selection still follows the visible list; only the focus stays put. | bug fix |

The pill's label changes shape ("Notes (1)" for "All Notes & Logs (2)") but nothing marks the view as narrowed, so returning to the page later it passes for the whole. Now the listing's "All Leads" pill, the Notes tab's "All Notes & Logs" pill and Qanvast Ops › Leads' pill carry `AppliedDot` (`components/om/applied-dot.tsx`) whenever their value is not the default: the DS Badge (dot), pinned to the pill's top-right corner and **tinted primary at 8px with a 2px surface ring** so it is the Filters count badge's (REG-69) own grammar at dot size — the DS dot's 6px red would read as an alert beside it. A hidden ", filter applied" follows the label for assistive tech. The search box needs no mark: its own text is the indicator. | user-directed |

| REG-297 | Cross | **Scope pill or Filters — the rule, and the tabs stay as they are** (user 2026-09-02, after asking whether Tasks / Quotes / Contracts / Questionnaire should take the Notes scope pill, or Notes should move under Filters — "so its better to leave as is today?" → yes). **One dominant single-choice dimension → a scope pill** that names the value and the count and wears `AppliedDot` off its default (the listing's stage, Notes' kind, Qanvast Ops' status). **Several dimensions, or any multi-select → the Filters icon + modal**, selected state + primary count badge whenever anything is applied (REG-69: Tasks, Quotes, Contracts). **Never both for one dimension** — a pill must not duplicate a modal group, so status stays inside the Tasks / Quotes / Contracts modals and no pill is added there; Notes keeps its pill because kind is its only dimension and the mock drew it as chips (931:44884). | REG-298 | Cross | **The mobile shell is the mock's** (user 2026-09-02: "we started designing the mobile responsive mocks" — 1082:23305 "Lead Listing", the first mobile mock; supersedes the REG-269 chrome). ≤767: a **60px top bar** (1082:23389) — the lockup left; search, chat, notifications (24 Light) and the 36px avatar (r8, 1px surface/variant stroke) right — and a **fixed 70px bottom dock** (1082:24937): Dashboard · Leads · Tools · Projects · More as 64×50 columns (36px icon box r8, subtitle/xs label), the active route's box tinted primary/variant with the filled glyph. | REG-301 | Lead Details | **Every Lead Details tab painted blank under its header at ≤767** (found verifying REG-298; present since REG-269 — the banked `notes-toolbar-mobile.png` shows it). The L2 header goes `position: static` at mobile to leave the sticky stack, but its two absolutely positioned background plates (`::before` canvas, `::after` white) then lost their containing block, positioned against the page-reveal container and covered the whole page below the header; the content was there (hit-testable, opaque) but unseen. Fix: `position: relative` at mobile — the plates stay inside the header, and relative is just as un-sticky. The header also takes the mock's level-1 lift there (REG-298). | bug fix |
| REG-302 | Cross | **The FAB sits 16 above the review banner at every width** (user 2026-09-02: "fab has to be above the floating container right" — at mobile it overlapped the banner). The banner now writes `--bottom-dock` from its measured height (ResizeObserver, re-measured as it wraps) and the FAB raises by `--bottom-dock + 26` = 16 + height + 16; the snackbar, bulk bars, AI sheet and upload toast read the same variable. Desktop is unchanged to the pixel (50 → 56 → 82). | bug fix |

A toggle beside the pencil in the name row ("Collapse / Expand lead details" — not "lead info", which is a section's own name) — the rail's `left_panel_close` ≥768, the sections' own up/down chevron ≤767 — folds the whole column. **≥768 collapsed** = the Create strip's twin on the left: 72 wide, p24, `left_panel_open` toggle · 24×1 rule · one 24px glyph per block (person Contact · people_alt Collaborators · info Lead info · home Property info · sticky_note_2 Other info), tooltips to the right; a glyph expands the column, opens that section and scrolls it under the sticky tabs. **≤767 collapsed** = the identity block alone, the sections folded (the column stacks there). Remembered per browser (`om.detail.infoColumn`, the quick-links store pattern); default = the mock's expanded column on desktop and tablet, collapsed on a phone. Width animates on the rail's timing after a toggle only. | REG-306 | Cross | **Responsive audit against the owner's brief (2026-09-02) — measured at 320 · 360 · 390 · 768 · 1024 · 1025 · 1199 · 1200 · 1440, fixes for what broke.** The brief's floor is 320, which REG-269 never verified. Found and fixed: (1) the bottom dock's five 64px columns spilled 8px at 320 — they now share the width (max 64); (2) the Lead Details header's action cluster clipped Convert to Project at 320 and the kebab at 360 — at ≤767 it takes its own full line under the title and wraps; (3) the imports toolbar's controls overflowed at 320 — they wrap under the search; (4) the listing card's task box (38) and the top bar's avatar (36) take 40px touch boxes; (5) **iOS input zoom**: Safari zooms when a focused field's text is under 16px and the DS fields set 14 — on iOS only (`@supports (-webkit-touch-callout: none)`), at ≤767, form controls type at 16px; nothing else changes and pinch zoom stays free (device-verify; Playwright cannot). False positives ruled out: Qanvast Ops' table scrolls inside its card with the Name column frozen; the Settings chip row's part-cut chip is the scroll affordance (REG-269). Left open, logged in `design-source/context/responsive.md` § Brief mapping: 40 vs 44px targets, touch growth on tablets (the REG-273 rules are gated on width, so an iPad at 768 keeps 20px table controls), the 1025 desktop threshold, 16px body at mobile, safe-area insets, card layouts for the other tables; the mobile ghost is closed by REG-309. | bug fix |
| REG-307 | Cross | **Touch growth reaches touch tablets** (user 2026-09-02 "go ahead" on the REG-306 audit's open item). Every REG-273 rule that exists only to enlarge a touch target — 40px min sizes, padding paid back with negative margin, `::after` hit areas, rows and heads grown to fit 40px boxes — used to live under `@media (max-width: 767px)` alone, so an iPad at 768–1199 kept the desktop's 20px table controls (measured: copy-phone icons 14×14, stage buttons 93×20, kebabs 24 and 20). Those rules now carry the media query LIST `@media (max-width: 767px), (hover: none) and (max-width: 1199px)` across 42 stylesheets; mixed blocks were split so the mobile LAYOUT rules (wrapping, stacking, widths, the 24 → 16 inset) stay on the bare 767 query. Desktop with a mouse and tablet with a mouse are byte-identical on every route; on a touch tablet at 768 every control on the listing, overview, tasks, files and notes now measures ≥40 (the Files folder card's open button, 32 inside its 56 card, got a full-card hit area on the way). Done as a ten-batch agent migration, each batch adversarially reviewed against the rule, then a completeness sweep over every stylesheet. Two tooltip fallbacks that show a caption because the DS Tooltip never fires on a touch pointer (the file viewer's zoom-fit label, the Lead Stages card's "why" caption on a blocked row) travel with the touch query too — the sweep flagged them as borderline and they were kept, since an iPad is exactly the case they exist for. The floor stays 40 (REG-273); 44 is one number away if wanted. | user-directed |
| REG-308 | Cross | **iOS safe areas** (user 2026-09-02 "go ahead" on the audit's open item). `viewport-fit: cover` (app/layout.tsx) lets the page reach under the notch and the home indicator, and the mobile chrome pads itself back out: `--app-mobile-bar-height` = 60 + `env(safe-area-inset-top)`, `--app-mobile-dock-height` = 70 + `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)`, so every sticky offset, the FAB, the snackbar, the review banner and the page clearance move with the inset; the bar and the dock spend the inset as padding (top / bottom) and both pad off a landscape notch (left / right); the More sheet's 24 bottom and the full-screen sheets' pinned footers (and their heads, for the status bar) add the inset too. Every `env()` has a `0px` fallback, so nothing moves where there is no inset — Playwright renders byte-identical. `usableViewportHeight` now measures the dock element instead of parsing the token (a custom property holding `calc()` + `env()` does not resolve through `getPropertyValue`). Device verification still to do. | user-directed |
| REG-309 | Listing | **The listing's ghost mirrors the mobile cards** (user 2026-09-02 "go ahead" on the REG-299 known gap). `LeadsSkeleton` ships both layouts and `skeleton.module.css` picks one by breakpoint, because loading.tsx is server-rendered and cannot read the width in JS: ≥768 the table bones exactly as before; ≤767 the header's three 36px squares, a pill + a square in the toolbar, and a column of card bones (name + age, info line, task box, rule, avatar + member square + stage bone) on the surface/bg canvas, 16 apart. | user-directed |
| REG-310 | Listing | **Row actions sit in flow on a touch pointer** (found by the REG-307 verification at 768 under touch). The listing's Edit + kebab pair is a zero-width sticky span floating over the visible right edge, revealed on hover (779:24696) — and REG-273 makes it always-on where there is no hover, so on a touch tablet it covered the Tasks column at every scroll position. Under `(hover: none)` the pair is now an in-flow last cell (row padding 8 / 16, the head carries a 134px spacer so its rule spans the row) that the row scrolls to, the way a last column would; a mouse keeps the mocked hover float. The Tasks, Quotes and Contracts tables float the same pair absolutely at right 24 and covered their last column the same way: on a touch pointer up to 1199 their rows gain a 150px band past the last cell (a flex spacer on Tasks, an extra grid track on the Quotes and Contracts cards), the Files table's own REG-273 answer generalised; the listing's floor width grows by the band from the one place that computes it (`table.tsx`, `TOUCH_TABLET` in lib/breakpoint.ts), so every row and the head share one width. Desktop and tablet-with-mouse are byte-identical. | bug fix |
| REG-311 | Tasks | **Tasks rows share one width at ≤1199** (found by the REG-310 verification; present since REG-269). The tablet scroller gave every row `width: max-content`, so a row with a long title was wider than its neighbours and the columns drifted apart at the end of the sideways scroll — with a mouse too. Rows and the head now share a 1244px floor, the mock's own card width, so the columns sit exactly where the desktop puts them (title 422 beside 822 of fixed columns); on a touch pointer the floor carries the REG-310 band as well. The tablet render changes on purpose. | bug fix |
| REG-314 | Cross | **Body text reads 16px at mobile** (owner 2026-09-02, choosing the brief's "minimum readable body text of 16px" over the mobile mock's 14 and over the audit's advice). ≤767 the DS `--om-type-body-m` token is redefined in app/globals.css to font-size l (16) on a 24px line, so every value, paragraph, input, list line and link that composes body/m follows; body/s, captions, labels, buttons and headings keep their sizes. Consequences to expect: one-line rows on the 20px rhythm grow to 24, and long values wrap a line earlier. The desktop is untouched. | user-directed |
| REG-315 | Cross | **Desktop starts at 1025; tablet is 768–1024** (owner 2026-09-02, adopting the brief's ranges over the audit's advice). Every `@media (max-width: 1199px)` became `(max-width: 1024px)` (104 rules across 73 files, plus `TABLET_DOWN` and `TOUCH_TABLET` in lib/breakpoint.ts and the comments that named the range), so from 1025 the desktop layout renders: the rail may pin open, the Create strip may expand, the info column is 360 and the overview cards sit in two columns. At 1025 the fixed chrome leaves 493px for the centre with the rail collapsed and the strip closed (two cards at ~235), less with either open; the owner has seen the numbers. Verified for overflow at 1025 and 1100. | user-directed |
| REG-312 | Cross | **The touch-target floor is 44, not 40** (owner 2026-09-02, adopting the brief's 44×44; REG-273 had set 40). Every touch-ergonomics rule — 176 touch blocks across 90 stylesheets — was re-done with the mark unchanged and the box at 44: negative margins recomputed as −(44 − mark)/2 (a 24 glyph: −10, a 20: −12, a 16: −14), link paddings paid back at 12 on a 20px line, `::after` hit areas at −(44 − drawn)/2 (a 36 control: −4, a 38: −3), and rows or heads that exist to hold a box at 44. Done as an eleven-batch agent migration with an arithmetic review per batch and a completeness sweep over every block; the sweep's six leftovers were applied by hand. Rows that grew from 40 to 44 move the mobile and touch-tablet renders by design; desktop with a mouse is untouched. | user-directed |

The props, the `data-drawer` attribute, its CSS and the drawer comments are removed; the in-flow rail is unchanged. | user-directed |

(1) In "Select OpenMaison Field Name" a field another column already holds is disabled (the menu's own disabled state) and carries "Mapped to {imported column}" at its right, so a collision is explained where it happens; Ignore is never taken, and a row's own value stays selectable. The REG-295 dot goes with the pill; a selected chip is its own applied state. | REG-322 | Cross | **A modal footer's shadow follows the scroll** (user 2026-09-04: "if the modal window height is within viewport then the modal window footer containers will not have shadow but just a top border. If the height is exceeding and have internal scrolls then add shadow. The current border and shadow style is correct"). Every modal footer keeps its top rule; the shadow (`--om-shadow-modal-footer`, the value unchanged) shows only while the body above it actually scrolls, and leaves when it stops. One hook — `useOverflow` (components/om/use-overflow.ts) — watches the scrolling body with a ResizeObserver on it and its children (an expanding section, a row added or a shrinking viewport flips it without a commit) and the footer takes `data-shadow`. Applied to the shared Dialog shell and every hand-rolled modal: Create / Edit Lead (its shadow used to follow the Additional info toggle, REG-38, and now follows the measured scroll, so a short viewport shows it collapsed too), the four Filters modals, the Move stage window (its stage panel), the Return Lead wizard, Move to folder (keeps its own 0 25 50 value), Attach files, the task-template dialogs and the Review imported leads panes — those last four had no shadow before. The Import wizard's footer takes the shadow only when a step body scrolls (step 1 at ≤1024): its mapping and preview tables scroll inside their own bordered boxes above the footer, nothing runs under it, so at desktop it is a rule where the mock (780:92903) drew a shadow. The Task / Comments Drawer is a side panel, not a modal window, and keeps its always-on footer shadow. **Amended the same day** (user: "some issue with the shadow"): in the Move stage window the shadow showed over the rail but not the stage panel — the panel sits in a positioned, animated wrapper, which paints after a static footer and covered it (the review sheet's sticky table columns and the Import wizard's positioned body would do the same). Every footer is now `position: relative; z-index: 1`, the layer Move to folder already used. | user-directed |
| REG-323 | Listing | **The review queue scrolled sideways by 1px** (user 2026-09-04: "there is a horizontal scroll which is not required"). The queue column is 288 wide including its 1px divider, so its content box is 255 — and the card was a fixed 256 (the comment's "16 + 256 + 16" forgot the border): one pixel too wide for an `overflow-y: auto` column, which is all a horizontal scrollbar needs, and with the classic scrollbars a Mac shows while a mouse is attached the vertical bar takes another 15. The card now stretches to the column's content box (255 at desktop, narrower when a bar takes room), as it already did at ≤1024. **Same cause on Imports**: the history table's wrapper is 1316 outside its 1px borders at 1440, the mock's columns (779:20147) sum to that same 1316 — so the table was 2px wider than the box and scrolled sideways by 2px. The wrapper's rule is now an inset outline (the mock's inside stroke) rather than a border, so the content box is the full 1316 and every column keeps its number. | bug fix |

Now every write from the panel answers with the snackbar the app already uses: **"Task updated"** (name · description · due date), **"Tags updated"** (the panel's Add Tag picker and the table's tag popover alike), **"File removed"** (an attachment's Remove), **"Due date updated"** (the due picker, which the table's Due column shares — as "Members updated" already shows from either place). | REG-329 | Cross | **A pass over the small things** (user 2026-09-04: "when I hover on a member avatar their names should show up in a tooltip … check similar use cases and fix across", "please go through across in detail and fix these issues once for all"). Found by an audit that crawled every page at rest and with its popovers, menus, modals and panels open, checking native title bubbles, icon-only controls without the DS tooltip, clipped single-line text, link-styled controls wider than their text, pointer targets that do not answer a hover, and cards reachable only by a chevron. Fixed: (1) **Avatars name their member** — a lone avatar (member stacks in the listing, cards, kanban, the task table and panel) shows the DS tooltip with the name and exposes it as `role="img"`; where the name is printed beside it (pickers, filter lists, creator / owner cells, the info column) the caller passes `tooltip={false}`; the native `title` is gone. (4) **Two more silent saves confirm**: the Quotes due picker ("Due date updated") and the inline task rename ("Task updated"), as REG-326. (5) **Names are always "Farah Ahmad"** (user: "whatever case the user types … first letter in caps"): `titleCaseName` (lib/format.ts) capitalises every word and lowers the rest — after a space, hyphen or apostrophe — on both write paths (the lead form and the import) and at read time for rows saved before today. (6) **The rail scrolls on a short viewport** (user: "the sidebar looks messed up … there should be simple scroll inside the sidebar", then "let the whole thing be scrollable instead of just the top menus so that everything is upfront and visible"): the rail body — Search, the nav, Upgrade, the bottom items and the studio row — scrolls as one under a fixed logo row, which takes level-1 only while the body is scrolled beneath it (user: "fix the logo on scroll and add the shadow only in this view and when there is scroll"); the two blocks keep the items' 8px between them (user: "equal vertical spacing"). The column used to shrink every block, so the lockup sat over Search and Upgrade over Company. (7) **The studio owner reads "Dane Bruce Lee"** (user), in the rail footer and the mobile bar's avatar label; the member record "Dane" is unchanged. Judged not defects: the skip link (hidden on purpose), Settings' 240-wide tab rows and full-width menu items (rows by design), the DS text field's empty `title`, and hover on a text field (the DS draws none). | user-directed |
| REG-330 | Shell | **The rail neither expands nor collapses below the desktop breakpoint** (user 2026-09-04: "the sidebar looks broken … if there are smaller screens then we shouldn't allow expand collapse right"). At 768–1024 the REG-269 rule forced only the WIDTH back to 52: a rail pinned open on desktop (`om.rail.expanded`) still rendered its expanded styling — 200-wide items, labels and the search box at full opacity, `--rail-w` at 216 — clipped inside 52px, so a letter of every label peeked out beside its icon and the content offset was wrong. Now the component treats the rail as collapsed whenever the viewport is tablet-and-down: no labels, tooltips on, `--rail-w` 52, and the logo tile is a plain logo (its expand action and tooltip are off); the saved preference is left alone and resumes at ≥1025. The tablet stylesheet also neutralises the expanded styling, so nothing shows before the media query resolves on the first client paint. | user-directed |
| REG-331 | Shell | **Page headers take their shadow only on scroll** (user 2026-09-04: "where is the shadow on scroll", after the rail's logo row got the rule). The sticky L1 and L2 headers keep the hairline at the top of a page and on a page that fits; once the window has scrolled, level-1 joins the inside stroke on the plate (`useWindowScrolled`, components/shell/use-window-scrolled.ts). On Lead Details the sticky tab strip sits under the L2 header and carries the mock's own level-1 (858:24784), so the header there stays on the hairline (`scrollShadow={false}`) — one shadow at the bottom of the stuck stack, never two. ≤767 keeps REG-298's permanent level-1. | user-directed |
| REG-332 | Listing | **The stage cell opens its menu from anywhere in the cell — and the menu is back** (user 2026-09-04: "make the whole cell clickable and the dropdown is missing"). Two things: (1) REG-329's tooltip on the stage button had put a zero-size wrapper between the button and its cell, and the menu was anchored to `parentElement` — the wrapper — so it positioned against nothing and never showed while the cell drew its open ring. Bug, introduced 2026-09-04, fixed the same day. (2) The click now lives on the cell itself, which is also the anchor (780:86452), so the whole 64px cell is the target; the button inside stays for the keyboard and its click bubbles up, so nothing fires twice. The mobile cards and the kanban were unaffected: they anchor to the button. | bug fix + user-directed |

| REG-334 | Move stage | **A lead still in New assigns its members inside the window, and New is checked only once it has one** (user 2026-09-04: from the Lead stages card, "clicking here opens the stage flow modal, but can't seem to add member. Additive migration 0012: `assigned_at` on `lead_assignees`; `writeAssignees` is now a diff (members who stay keep their stamp, newcomers are stamped, the rest go) instead of a wipe-and-rewrite; the feed reads "You assigned <member> to this lead." with a person_add glyph, and rows from before the column read as assigned when the lead was created. **Still not recorded**, because no row survives them: a member's removal, an edit to the lead's own fields, a deleted task or note, a reverted stage.
