The Contacted journey's Suggested tasks dialog opened with its suggestion pre-ticked, so Save added a task nobody chose. The suggestion now starts unticked in the Move stage window, as the Notes tab's Auto Fill dialog already did; the auto-added reminder from the schedule pair is unchanged. | user feedback via the owner |
| REG-554 | Company · Templates | **The brand kit lives under Templates** (owner 2026-09-23: "since this is part of quotes and contract templates why is this outside of the templates?" → "inside the template is better"). **Brand kit** is the Templates strip's first tab (Brand kit · Quotes · Contracts · Tasks · T&Cs & Notes · Payments; Templates still lands on Quotes, REG-500): the logo, stamp, badges, six colours and four type roles with the default template's first page redrawing as you edit, Discard / Save changes in the header (`components/company/templates/brand-kit-view.tsx`, `/company/templates/brand`, honours `?from=`). **Company › Profile keeps the company details** (name, UEN, address, phone, fax, email, website, GST reg, licence) with the same live preview, and a card that leads to the kit. Both write the one `brand` record. The template editor's Brand tab links "Edit the brand kit" to the new page with the editor as its origin (Back returns); the inspector's badge notes and the Company panel's help lines ("Company details" · "Brand kit, quotes, contracts and more") follow. Reason it sat under Profile before (REG-484 / 487): the kit is read by every document kind, so it went one level up as the studio's identity — but it is the templates' shared design layer, and that is where a studio owner looks for it. | user-directed |
| REG-555 | Quote editor | **Print as — the word in place of the amount** (the testers' sheet, Q-1 + T-3; owner 2026-09-23: "build rest of the things"). A line's row menu carries **Print as ›** — *The price* · FOC · Inclusive · KIV · On behalf · Packaged · *Custom word…* — each with its counting rule under it: FOC, Inclusive, KIV, Packaged and a custom word print the word where the amount would and leave every total (FOC / Inclusive / Packaged work still costs the studio, so their cost meets the owner's margin); **On behalf** keeps the amount, counts in the subtotal and carries no GST. The grid shows the word bold in the Total cell with the line's own figure under it ("S$120.00 · not counted") and a tag after the name; the bulk bar's **Print as** marks a whole run through a small dialog; the price stays on the line, so *The price* brings it back. In print the word stands in the amount column (the zero-amount word's weight) and the unit price is blank; the template's Zero amounts stays the fallback for a S$0 line with no word. **A section's sub-total can carry a word** (T-3): row menu › *Label the sub-total…* ("Approximate") prints "Sub-total for Masonry (Approximate)" and shows under the section's sum in the grid. Columns `print_as` · `print_as_text` on `quote_items` (migration 0028); rules in `lib/quotes/math.ts` (`counts` · `bearsCost` · `printsWord`), the vocabulary in `lib/quotes/items-model.ts`. Copy invented. | user-directed |
| REG-556 | Quote editor | **Highlight — a kit colour on a line or a section** (Q-2). In the grid the row wears a 4px bar in the colour on its left edge; an ink colours the name and description, a fill tints the row. In print an ink colours the row's words, a fill its cells; a highlighted section colours its band. Whole lines only, no free colours (the audit's recommendation). | REG-557 | Quote editor | **Formatted descriptions** (Q-3). Save keeps both the HTML (`description_html`) and its plain text (`description`, bullets as "• " lines — search, export, X-Ray and the one-line cell keep working); text with no formatting stays plain, as before. A formatted description shows its plain text with the bullets in the row and opens the dialog on a press — the 24px box cannot hold the shape. In print the description keeps its lists and emphasis (`.rich`), or its first line when the template asks for that; a section note prints the same way under the band. | user-directed |
| REG-558 | Quote editor | **Sections inside sections, the nested numbering, no-number lines, Ungroup** (Q-4 · Q-10 · Q-6). A section may hold sections one level down: *Section with n items* on lines that share a section makes a **Sub-section** there (tagged so, inset once more, a darker band); *Move to section* lets a section without sub-sections join a top one; a section asked for inside a sub-section lands beside it. The grid numbers 1 · 1.1 · 1.1.1 (`numberItems` is recursive), a folded section hides everything under it, its sums and Declined answer reach every descendant, Duplicate copies the tree, Remove says "Remove this section? … its n lines leave — to keep the lines, use Ungroup". **Ungroup — keep the lines** dissolves a section and steps its lines (and sub-sections) up into its parent at its place; Undo (⌘Z) brings it back. **Skip the number / Number this line** marks a line `no_number`: the grid prints "–" and the count passes over it; in print the line's number cell is blank and the numbering skips it. The template's Item numbers gains **"1 · 1.1 · a b c"** (`numbering: 'nested'`): the section's number, its lines as 1.1 · 1.2, a sub-section as 1.3 with its lines lettered a · b · c; the other modes print a sub-section as a labelled row inside its section (the room-label row, numbered in the nested mode) with its own note and highlight. Server rules in `app/quotes/items-actions.ts` (`createQuoteItem` · `setQuoteItemParent` · `groupQuoteItems` · `ungroupQuoteItem` · `deleteQuoteItems` all walk descendants). | user-directed |

The maths takes them in order from what the additional discount left (`quoteTotals` → `discountLines`); the Totals block prints the additional discount on its own line and then **each named discount as its own line with its word** (the "Additional discount" line's switch governs them all; the label falls back to that line's when a discount has no word). | REG-564 | Data | **Migration 0028 and repair R36** (round 1 of the testers' asks). `quote_items` + `print_as` · `print_as_text` · `highlight` · `no_number` · `description_html`; `quotes` + `discounts_json` · `revised_at`; `members` + `email`; `contracts` + `signed_nric`; tables `quote_versions` (REG-565) and `price_book_sections` (REG-566), both in `BASELINE_TABLES`. R36 sets every member's email on the studio's domain, adds the studio as its first issuing entity to the brand record, and marks a zero-cost line on OM-10008 FOC where one exists (none does today — the rule stays for a re-seed); checks: member with no email, brand record with no entity, a custom Print as with no word, a section nested deeper than one level, a kept version at or above its quote. `npm run db:baseline` after the repair. Found by the round's responsive probe and fixed: the quote preview's action row (Internal copy · What your client sees · Copy link · Download PDF · Send) never shrank, so a phone scrolled 315px sideways (REG-490's bar) — at ≤767 it takes its own line and wraps. | bug fix |
| REG-565 | Quote editor | **Revisions** (Q-13). ⋯ › **Revise — new version** on a quote the client has seen (or the studio approved; a Draft never sent is edited, not revised — the snackbar says so) asks "Start version n+1?", then keeps the quote as it stands — fields, lines, payment rows — as a `quote_versions` snapshot and moves the row on: version n+1, a Draft again under the same number with the same lines, its sent / approved stamps cleared. The header's chip reads **Version 2**, Activity says "Version 1 kept and version 2 started by Dane", the lead's feed "Dane revised the quote OM-10008 — version 1 kept, version 2 started". ⋯ › **Version History** (coming soon until now) is a 420 drawer: a lead line, the Revise button (disabled with its reason on an unsent Draft), then the current version and every kept one with when · by whom · what it was · its amount and **Open** — the preview at `?version=n`: titled "Version n", a *Read-only* chip, a banner "Version 1 of 2 — read-only · Kept on … by … It was Approved at S$… Version 2 is the one being worked on" with *Open version 2*, Copy link and Download PDF, no Send and no client-view panel. The kept version renders through the quote's template like any other. `reviseQuote` in `app/quotes/actions.ts`; `fetchQuotePreview(quoteId, version)` reads the snapshot (`reviveSnapshot`); `components/quotes/editor/version-history.tsx`. | user-directed |

The grid says what the templates say: the row tag **Section** (a nested one **Sub-section**), the toolbar's **New section** (was Create Group), Create ▾ › New section, row menu *Section with n items* · *Move to section* · *No section*, the bulk bar's *Section*, the snackbars ("Section created", "Moved into the section"), the regroup dialog's "Keep my sections", a new section named "New section". The free label the mock also calls Section (the Category · Room · Section column, 1271:41169) keeps its column head and reads **Section label** where the two could meet — View by, Add Section label, the template's Group by. | user-directed |
| REG-568 | Quote editor | **The edge add and the Expand button collided on an active row** (owner 2026-09-23, screenshot: "there is button conflict"). The Expand icon hangs under the description box's right end (REG-478: 50–74px in the 72px row) and the edge add rides the row's bottom edge at the pointer's x (REG-527: 61–83px), so with the caret in the description and the pointer near the box's end the primary + sat over the Expand. An active row now hides its edge add and edge line (`.row[data-active] .edgeAdd`); the leading + and the row menu stay, and the edge add returns the moment the row rests. No other geometry moved. | bug fix |
| REG-569 | Quote template editor | **The second visual pass, and plainer words** (owner 2026-09-23, with a screenshot of the Details rows: "a lot of things looks broken in the quotes template editor and preview … go through the whole flow component by component and div by div across viewports and fix all the visual issues and simplify the ui … it's more sounding like technical language … too techie for a old studio owner"). A screenshot sweep of every state at 1440 · 1280 · 1025 · 768 · 390 (`tpl-sweep.cjs`, 49 shots) found: **(1) the Details rows and the Items columns had squeezed their value / heading boxes to a sliver** — a label, a value, a field button and ▲ ▼ ✕ on one 288px line. Each is now a **card on two lines**: the label (or the switch and the column's name) with its move / remove buttons, then the value with its field menu (or the printed heading, its width and alignment). **(2) The Insert field menu was clipped by the pane's edge** — it is a portal now, and every value box that carries a field says what it prints as on the quote on the page ("Prints as: Mr & Mrs Tan"), so the words in braces stop reading as code. **(3) A row added to the header's Details box printed over "Dear Sir / Mdm" and the body** — canvases lay their content out now: an element whose height comes from its words (text, details, signature, badges, page number) pushes down whatever starts inside it (a box or a picture is a background and pushes nothing), and the header or footer grows to hold everything, the body starting under its real bottom (`components/templates/render/canvas.tsx`, the document's `canvasH`). The stored positions never change — a pushed element is only drawn lower — so the print matches the editor. **(4) The Letter house style's footer printed the signature over the studio's address** (the address sat at 16 mm under a ~20 mm signature): the preset moves it to 22 mm in a 36 mm footer and repair R37 moves every stored copy of that design the same way. **(5) The top bar clipped a chip mid-word and lost the saved stamp below 1440** — the stamp sits after the chips and keeps its width, and below 1320 the chips wrap under the name. **(6) The quote preview's bar at 768 broke its template chip into three lines over the title** — the chip stays on one line and the actions take their own line up to 1024. **(7) Half-column selects and numbers truncated their words** ("Same as th…", "Label width (m…"): the font select spans the pane, the Details box's two numbers are full width. **Plainer words** (the owner's worry, and the design lead's call: the editor is for a studio owner, not a layout engine): the left tabs are **Page · Brand · New quotes** (were Structure · Brand · Defaults); "Add block" is **Add to the page**; blocks read *Picture · Line across · Blank space · New page* (were Image · Divider · Spacer · Page break); a canvas lists what is *on the header* and offers *Add to the header*; the element head reads "Details in the header" (was "Details · Header"); Rule → Line, weight → thickness, "Keep with the next block" → "Keep together with what follows", "Group by / Then by" → "Group the items by / Then split each by", "Section bands" → "Section headings", "Header row" → "Column headings", "Zebra rows" → "Shade every other row", "Zero amounts" → "A line that costs nothing prints", "Mark" → "Above the line", "Type" → "Fonts" (the Brand tab and the kit page), "Right block" → "On the right", "Value lines: None / Underlined (form)" → a switch "Underline the values, like a form", "Page setup · A4 · 12/18/10/18 mm" → "Page setup · A4". Probes `verify-tpl-audit.cjs` (9) + `verify-template-editor.cjs` (28) rerun on the renamed words; screenshots in `design-source/verify/template-editor/pass-2/`. Later the same day: the preview's template chip, made `nowrap` here, overflowed a 320 phone by 57px under a long template name — it caps at the row's width and its name ellipsizes (the 320 gate of `verify-round1-responsive.cjs`). | user-directed |
| REG-570 | Quote editor | **The Items section's heads hold while the table scrolls, and the sideways scrollbar stays in reach** (owner 2026-09-23: "while scrolling the item header can be sticky until it reaches the end of the container so that the item and table headers are visible in the viewport"; the Items-table UX audit, `design-source/context/quote-editor/03-items-table-ux-audit-2026-09-23.md` A1 · A2, built on the owner's "go ahead"). Measured before: after 700px of scroll the section top sat at −51 and the column heads at 17, both gone under the tab strip; the sideways scrollbar sat 1,924px below the heads. Cause: the head row lived inside the sideways scroller, and CSS sticky only holds to the nearest scrolling ancestor. Built: the section card's top holds (`SectionCard sticky`) at `--editor-sticky` (header 76 + tab strip 48 = 124; 48 at ≤767, where the header leaves the stack), and the column heads live in a bar of their own above the scroller (`.headBar`, sticky at 124 + the top's measured height — `--section-top-h` from a ResizeObserver, so a wrapping toolbar at 1025–1199 still lines up), scrolled sideways in step with the body (each follows the other's scrollLeft; the echo of a value one side set is recognised and dropped — it used to write a stale offset back into a body mid-way through a smooth pan and stop it); the frozen ITEM / TOTAL heads keep their places because the bar is a scroller too. Both let go when the card's end passes (sticky's own rule; measured: the top's bottom stays inside the card). A held head casts the 1px rule and a soft shadow (`useStuck`, `components/om/use-stuck.ts`, listening to every scroller on the page). In the full-page Expand the card's body is the scroller and the heads hold at its top. The native scrollbar is hidden; a 24px bar sticks to the viewport's bottom while the table runs on — a pager at each end, a track whose thumb is the visible share (drag the thumb, press the track, press a pager; the pagers hide under a finger) — and rests at the table's foot once the end is on screen; not drawn while the table fits. Verified: `verify-items-ux.cjs` (47) at 1440 / 768 touch / 390 touch; screenshots `design-source/verify/items-ux/`. | user-directed |
| REG-571 | Quote editor | **Rows drag with the pointer, like the Payment Schedule, and a line can be dropped into another section** (owner 2026-09-23: "drag and drop vertically and horizontally is not visually smooth transition like it is in the payments"; audit B1 · B3 — dropping into a section was the owner's yes on the audit's first question). Before: the browser's own HTML5 drag from the 20px handle — the ghost was the handle icon, a 2px line marked the slot, nothing slid, the row jumped into place after the server wrote, and a drop beside another parent was refused with a snackbar. Built (`components/quotes/editor/items/row-drag.ts`): the pressed row lifts (elevation 3) and follows the pointer — a section with everything under it as one block — riding a CSS variable (`--row-dy`), so no move re-renders a row; the rows it passes slide by its height (160ms); the slot follows the row under the pointer: its upper half = before it, its lower half = after it among ITS siblings, under an open section's title = first inside it, past the last row = the end of the table; the lifted row's ITEM cell indents to the slot's depth (flush · 32 · 56), so the eye knows where the line will land before it is let go; on release the block settles into its gap (170ms) and the order lands in the same render, never jumping back first. The lines move at once; the parent change (`setQuoteItemParent`) and the sibling order (`reorderQuoteItems`) are written after, a failed write reloads; "Moved into “Bedrooms”" / "Moved out of the section" when the parent changed; the dropped row pulses (REG-573). Refused slots keep the last good one: inside itself, a section into a sub-section, a section that holds sub-sections anywhere but the top (REG-558), a line into another View-by bucket. The window (or the Expand's scroller) scrolls while the pointer rides its edge; Escape cancels; the block never leaves the table's own height (the ghost stops at the first and last row, so the sideways scroller never grows a vertical bar). A finger drags too: the handle is back on touch (REG-273 had hidden it because HTML5 drag never starts from a finger), with a 32×44 box, the selection cell 80 (handle · checkbox); the page wears the grab cursor and nothing selects while a drag runs (`html.om-row-dragging`). Move to section stays in the row menu. Found on the tablet screenshot and fixed here: the row numbers walked the siblings in ARRAY order, so an optimistic reorder showed "5" above "4" until the next reload — `numberItems` orders by position now (lib/quotes/math.ts) and the drop keeps the array in position order. Probe: `verify-items-ux.cjs` — lift · slide · settle · written · into a section · Escape · a finger at 768. | user-directed |
| REG-572 | Quote editor | **A dragged column's body cells travel with its head** (owner 2026-09-23; audit B2). REG-462's head drag slid the heads while the body's cells swapped only on release. The column-drag hook writes the dragged head's offset to `--col-dx` on the table's `[data-drag-root]`, and `style(key)` now goes on every body cell of that column (rows, the sum row, the view buckets), so the whole column follows the pointer and the passed columns slide with their heads; the offset rides the variable, so a move never re-renders a row and React hears only when the head passes a neighbour. Every other table keeps its head-only drag, unchanged. | user-directed |
| REG-573 | Quote editor | **Every row that just arrived is brought into view and pulses; a new section takes the caret** (owner 2026-09-23: "when i add a new section it adds at the bottom and there is no auto scroll to the bottom"; audit C1 · C2). Measured before: a new section landed 2,529px down, out of view, the focus left on the toolbar button, the snackbar the only feedback. Built (`attention`, items-section.tsx): two frames after the reload the row is centred when it sits under the sticky heads or off the screen (`scrollIntoView` centre, the Expand's scroller too), pulses once (`data-fresh`, a 1.4s primary wash at 14%), and — New section from the toolbar, Create ▾ and the bulk bar, "Section with n items", a new line from Create ▾ / the leading + / the edge + / Add item above · below / a blank Add Item entry — its name holds the caret with the words selected, so typing replaces "New section" or "Item". Saved sections from My Sections, pasted lines, Duplicate, Move up / down, Move to section and a drag's drop reveal and pulse without taking the caret; an Add Item entry with a name typed keeps the caret in the field for the next line. | user-directed |
| REG-574 | Quote editor | **The free label's column reads "Section label"** (owner 2026-09-23 on the audit's D1). REG-567 named the grid's groups "Section", so the mock's SECTION column (1271:41169, the free label the Section-label view buckets by) shared a word with the SECTION row tag; an owner who typed a label expecting the line to move into a section got nothing. The head reads SECTION LABEL, the empty cell "Add label ▾", the menu's title, Set value's field, the paste mapping and the export header say "Section label" (View by already did). One word away from the mock, for the collision REG-567 made. | user-directed |
| REG-575 | Quote editor | **The row menu's mark rests faint on every row** (owner 2026-09-23 on the audit's D3, over the hover-only calls of REG-446 / REG-527). A bare ⋮ at 45 % sits on every row; the hovered row, an open menu or the keyboard draw the full pill (white, level-1, the primary border) — an owner who never sweeps the mouse still sees that every row has a menu; REG-446's 72px keeps the amount clear of it, so nothing moves. The edge + stays hover-only (it follows the pointer). Touch keeps its always-on kebab. | user-directed |
| REG-576 | Quote editor | **Three small reads for an older hand and eye** (audit D2 · D4 · D6, 2026-09-23). The section row's sub-total label (REG-555, T-3) showed as a bare word under the sum — the owner's screenshot read "test" as a stray — and reads prints “test” now. The 20px marks keep their drawing and take invisible boxes under a mouse too: handle and + 28×44 (they sit 4 apart, so the boxes meet with 4 to spare, the REG-365 rule), the eye and the fold 28×32 (they must not reach the name box beside them), the edge + 30; the touch rules grow them further. The sum row says "Sum · 19 priced items" — the title's count is every line, this one the lines that price. Not built from the audit: word labels on the toolbar's icon buttons (D5, the mock draws icons and the owner said not now) and muting the section rows' label prompts (D7, not asked). | user-directed · bug fix |
| REG-577 | Quote templates | **A template just published no longer reads "Unpublished changes", and edits after a publish read as one story** (owner 2026-09-23, a card reading "Unpublished changes" over "Published v1 · just now by Dane": "unpublished and published it has both the status"). Two things were wrong. **A false draft**: Publish clears the draft on the server, but the editor's autosave timer (900ms after the last edit) races the flush Publish does and could land a draft write after the publish — a draft equal to what had just been published, which the card read as "Unpublished changes" off `draft_json` alone; and the line's "just now" was the draft's stamp, not the publish's. `saveTemplateDraft` now stores no draft when the design and defaults equal the published ones (canonicalised through the parsers), and the gallery's `hasDraft` compares the draft with the published design + defaults, so a stale row reads right too. **The words**: a real draft over a published version no longer says "Published" and "Unpublished" on one card — the ribbon reads **Edited since v1**, the line "Published v1 · 3 days ago · edited just now by Dane" (the publish's stamp first, then the edit's), the table's status note "edited since v1", the editor's chip "Edited since v1", its kebab "Discard the edits since v1" and the confirm "Discard the edits since v1? … The edits since are lost." Verified by `verify-template-status.cjs` (an identical draft written by hand, a differing one, and the editor's own edit → publish → edit → undo path) and the editor suite rerun. | bug fix · user-directed |
| REG-578 | Quote templates | **The New quotes tab says where its settings go, and the signature switch says what it does** (owner 2026-09-23: "signature turned off and still it is showing in the quote editor"; the audit `design-source/context/quote-templates/03-defaults-to-quote-audit-2026-09-23.md` findings 1 · 3 · 9 · 10, built on "go ahead and fix everything" with my recommendations). The tab's intro reads "A new quote from this template starts with these — once you publish. Quotes you already have keep their own settings; a Draft quote can take these from its template card (Apply defaults)", and while the working copy differs from what is published a primary-ink line says "Not published yet — quotes take these once you press Publish changes" (a never-published draft: "… once you press Publish"). "Client signature required" was read as required-or-not; off has always meant the client signs the printed quote (the quote's own box says "Not requested — the client signs on paper", and the printed lines are the Page tab's Signature block, REG-421), so the switch reads **Client signs online** on the template and on the quote's Signature section, with "Off: the client signs the printed quote. The signature lines themselves are on the Page tab." under it; the picker's and the template card's words say "· signs online" / "· signs on paper" instead of dropping the subject when off. The printed client signature line stays the template's design (the owner's answer to the audit's first question). Verified by `verify-template-defaults.cjs`. | user-directed · invented copy |
| REG-579 | Quote templates | **A quote moved to a newer template version is asked before it takes the new settings; a sent quote is never moved** (audit finding 2 · 7). "Publish and update OM-…" from the editor opened on a Draft, and the banner's "Update to vN", moved the pinned version only while the snackbar said "OM-10001 updated" — the changed defaults never landed. Now the version moves and the quote asks "Apply defaults · Keep mine" (the Change-template question, REG-538): the editor returns to the quote with `?apply=<template>` (`withApply`, lib/nav.ts), the quote editor opens the question on arrival for a Draft on that template and drops the flag from the address so a reload never asks again; the banner's Update asks the same after the move; the snackbars say what happened ("v3 published — OM-10001 moved to v3", "Quote moved to v3"). The banner shows on Drafts only — an Approved, Sent or Signed quote keeps its version and its card still says "v1 · v2 published" — and `updateQuoteTemplateVersion` refuses past Draft, the publish dialog's own promise. Its "Move open draft quotes to this version" switch says "Their own settings stay as they are". | user-directed · bug fix |
| REG-580 | Quotes | **A Blank Quote starts with the studio default template's settings** (audit finding 4). It printed through the default template but took the editor's built-in settings — GST on, signature, 30 days, the 10/30/30/30 split, the table's default columns — so settings put on the default template did nothing for the modal's first tile. `createQuote` with no template now reads the default published template's New quotes (split, validity, GST, signature, texts, working columns) with an empty table (no starter sections); the tile reads "Blank Quote — the default template's settings, an empty table", the picker's "Empty table, the studio default's look and settings", the quote's template card "Prints through the studio's default template and started with its settings. Pick one to start from its sections." | user-directed |
| REG-581 | Quote templates | **A deleted text or payment template never seeds a quote, and leaves every template's New quotes** (audit finding 5). A Terms / Notes text deleted from its library (soft, REG-538) kept seeding new quotes and Apply defaults (`createQuote` read it regardless of `deleted_at`), the New quotes select showed a blank value for it, and a deleted split fell back to the default silently with the same blank. Now `createQuote` / `applyTemplateDefaults` ignore deleted texts; deleting a text clears it from every quote template's published and draft New quotes (`clearTextFromTemplateDefaults`), deleting a split writes the studio default in its place — what the delete dialogs already promised; and a New quotes select whose id is gone reads the fallback the quote would get ("None — write them on the quote", "Default: 10/30/30/30"), never a blank. | bug fix |
| REG-582 | Quote editor | **Save as template carries the quote's validity and, when asked, its sections** (audit finding 6). The dialog promised "this quote's payment split, validity, GST and signature settings as its defaults" but validity came from the quote's template; the new draft's validity is now Valid Until less the quote's date, in days, and a second switch "Also use its sections as the starter sections" (on) makes the quote's top-level sections the starter sections. Both paths (a quote on a template, a blank one) go through `carryQuoteExtras`. | bug fix · user-directed |
| REG-583 | Quote editor | **The GST (%) column rests while the quote's GST is off** (audit finding 8). Every line carried 9 % beside an off switch and the totals card read "GST 9%" next to it — a contradiction to an aged owner. The column leaves the Items table while GST is off and returns with the switch (the lines keep their rate, Column settings keeps its row); the card's label reads "GST" while off. | user-directed |
| REG-584 | Quotes | **Selling prices round to a step as they are entered** (the studios' tech-request sheet, rows 45 · 55, Cloud Creation — "all line-item prices in multiples of 5"; the proposal `design-source/context/feedback/02-tech-requests-quotes-templates-2026-09-23.md` P8, built on the owner's "go ahead and fix all of them"). Settings › Quotes › Selling prices: "Round selling prices to" Not rounded · the nearest S$1 · S$5 · S$10 (the studio rules key `quote_rules`, `lib/settings/types.ts` `QuoteRules` · `roundToStep`). With a step, a unit price typed or filled in the grid lands on it at once (the markup absorbs the difference, the cost never moves — Round Prices' own rule, REG-479), and a line added from My Items, pasted from a sheet or inserted from a saved section lands on it on the server. Round Prices still rounds a whole quote in one pass. | user-directed |
| REG-585 | Quotes | **A section prints as one line, and the client view chooses what is priced** (sheet rows 31 — Urban Home's blocker, "Tiling Works as a single selling-price line, three costed sub-items inside" — and 83, Dyel's lump-sum quoting; proposal P2). A section's row menu carries **Print as one line** (`quote_items.print_one_line`, migration 0029; the grid shows a "One line" tag): for the client the section folds into one priced line — its name, its description, its sum — at its own place in the print, while the studio keeps every line inside with its cost and margin; a folded sub-section folds into its section's line. Preview › What your client sees gains **Amounts**: *A price on every line* · *One amount per section* (the template's "One lump sum per section", now per quote) · *The total only* (no line amounts, no sub-totals — the totals block alone); the template's lump-sum switch sets the starting point (`ClientView.amounts`). | user-directed |
| REG-586 | Quotes | **A second number sequence for commercial work** (sheet row 12, Next Phase — "serial numbers for commercial and residential"; proposal P3). Row 32 (Urban Home's own sequence) was already REG-561. | user-directed |

Owners and Admins see the card as before. | user-directed |
| REG-590 | Quotes | **A backup approver** (sheet row 11, H Design; proposal P7). Settings › Quotes › Backup approver: a member picked beside the Owners and Admins. With approval required, **Approved is an approver's word**: the quote's status menu offers it to approvers only ("approvers only" beside it for anyone else) and the server refuses it from anyone else; the backup approver may also approve and publish templates (`isApprover`, lib/settings/queries.ts, in the template editor's review bar and `publishTemplate`). Before this nothing gated Approved. | user-directed · bug fix |
| REG-592 | Quote templates | **Dates print as the template says** (sheet row 97, Dyel — "Sunday, 7 February 2026", live in 1.0; proposal P9). Page setup › "Dates print as": 17 Sep ’26 · 17 September 2026 · Sunday, 17 September 2026 (`PageSettings.dateFormat`); `{date}` and `{valid_until}` follow it on the print, the canvas and the field previews. | REG-593 | Company | **An issuing entity carries its own GST registration** (sheet row 26, Dyel — four companies, one GST-registered; proposal P14). Company › Profile › each added entity has "GST-registered" (the studio's own stays Settings'); picking an entity on a quote brings its GST with it — the switch turns off with the word "GST off — Dyel Creative Pte Ltd is not GST-registered", the entity line under the select says so, and `{studio_gst}` prints nothing for an unregistered entity. | user-directed |
| REG-587 | Quotes | **Send for confirmation, then the T&Cs** (sheet rows 33 · 36, Mavid — "send for confirmation to the customer" before the agreement; proposal P4, owner 2026-09-23 "go ahead and fix all of them"). A new status **Confirmed** between Sent to Client and Signed (`QUOTE_STATUSES`; the Move stage window's signed-quote list accepts it). The editor's Send is a **menu** once the quote may go to the client — "Send for confirmation · The client confirms the quote on a link — no signature yet" / "Send for signature · Add the T&Cs and raise the agreement to sign"; while approval is the next step it stays the plain Send; once Confirmed the primary reads "Send for signature" (the contract picker). Send for confirmation opens the contract's send dialog with the quote's words (name · number · the WhatsApp draft · the link), the WhatsApp window opens on the press (REG-534's rule) and the quote is marked Sent to Client. The link `/confirm/[quoteId]` is the signing page's shell: the studio's letterhead, the quote exactly as its Preview prints it (its own client view, no internal marks), a card "Confirm this quote" with the total, the client's name and **Confirm quote** — the quote becomes Confirmed with the name and the stamp (`confirmed_at` · `confirmed_by`); already confirmed or signed, the card says so. Marking Confirmed by hand from the status menu stamps it too; the kebab copies the confirmation link; Activity reads "{name} confirmed the quote OM-… on its link". Copy of the page and the draft is invented. | user-directed · invented copy |
| REG-588 | Quotes | **A second client on the quote** (sheet row 47, Todz'terior "on hold" in 1.0; proposal P5). General Info › "Add a second client" reveals Second client · NRIC (second client) (`quotes.client2_name` · `client2_nric`, autosaved with the rest); the print's `{client_name}` reads "A & B" and `{nric}` "x / y" when the second is filled, and two new fields `{client_2_name}` · `{client_2_nric}` sit in the Insert field menu's Client group for a template that wants them apart. Empty, nothing changes. | user-directed |
| REG-591 | Contracts | **Several documents under one signing link** (sheet row 63, Mavid — "send all documents at once for signature", live in 1.0; proposal P16, parity). The Contracts tab's kebab gains **Send together for signature** (inert below two unsigned documents): a dialog on the Send for digital signature shell (REG-534) with a checklist of the lead's unsigned documents on top (all ticked), the client's name and number, the WhatsApp draft naming every document, the link. The bundle's id is made when the dialog opens, so the link is known on the press: WhatsApp opens at once, then `contract_bundles` records the send and every ticked document is marked Sent to Client under it (`contracts.bundle_id`; a bundled row's Copy Link copies the bundle's). The page `/sign/bundle/[id]` is the signing page with the documents stacked in order ("Document 1 of 2 · General Contract Agreement"), one card walking the three steps, and the one signature signing every document (each keeps its own signed copy and name). | user-directed |
| REG-594 | Company | **Company › Price book** (sheet rows 108 · 109 · 84 · 90, Todz'terior · IDL · Dyel · Mavid — "import our item list", "the same product from different vendors", "edit our packages"; proposal P11, parity). A Company section beside Templates on the panel, two tabs on the libraries' gallery (REG-538, generalised to take its shell and its noun): **My Items** — a card per item (the kind's glyph, the unit price, "by {vendor} · S$… / unit · category", "On n quotes" from `quote_items.price_book_item_id`) or the listing-model table (Item · Kind · Unit · Cost · Markup · Unit price · Category · Vendor · Used by · Updated); New ▾ = **New item** (a 600 dialog: name · description · kind · unit · cost · markup with the unit price under it · category · vendor) and **Import from a spreadsheet…** (paste the rows, an optional vendor for all of them, then REG-476's column mapping titled "Import into My Items"); the kind segments; search reads vendors and categories; Edit · Duplicate · Rename · Delete (soft — `deleted_at` on both tables, REG-502's rule; Deleted Items restores). **My Sections** — the sections saved from quotes (REG-566) as cards with their first lines, "n items · S$total", the same actions, **Edit** opening the section's own editor (name · description · the lines as Item · Qty · Unit price with move up / down / remove and Add a line, sub-sections under their own name); New section starts a blank one. The Add Item popover names the vendor ("S$1,100.00 / sqft · by Hafary") and finds items by it; **View Price Book** (coming soon since REG-415) opens the page with the way back. `price_book_items.vendor` lives; an inserted section stamps its group with the section's id for the usage count. Repair R38 names Floor Xpert on the vinyl, adds the same tile from Hafary and Soon Bee Huat, and saves OM-10008's Bathrooms as a section. | user-directed |
| REG-595 | Quote templates | **A revision prints its changes** (sheet rows 60 · 73, Urban Home — "the client should see what changed"; proposal P1, owner's answer: on by default). `lib/quotes/diff.ts` compares the kept version (`quote_versions`) with the current lines by id — added · removed · changed (name, qty, unit price, total). On a revision the Preview's client view gains **"Show changes from version n−1"** (on by default, with the counts): a removed line prints where it was, struck through in the kit's red and left out of the sums; an added line wears a small **New** mark; a changed line shows the old figure struck before the new one (the name, the qty — also in the combined "qty unit" cell — the unit price, the total). Off, version n prints clean. The client view's **Amounts** (REG-585) sits beside it. Version History's line reads "Since version 1: a added · r removed · c changed". Version 1 is untouched. | user-directed |
| REG-596 | Quotes | **Rebate, owners only** (sheet row 78, Mavid; proposal P13). A **Rebate** column on the lines (`quote_items.rebate_minor`, a vendor's rebate per unit) in the column catalogue for Owners alone: a Designer never sees it — not in the table, not in Column settings (the hidden columns keep their stored state), not in the export. The owner's margin strip counts it (the true cost = cost − rebate × qty, never below zero); nothing prints. Finance's rebate ledger stays Finance's. | user-directed |
| REG-597 | Quotes | **The Items table on a phone** (sheet row 68 — the owner's own ask; proposal P12; REG-417's open point). At ≤767 the grid gives way to **cards** — REG-377's card for a quote line: checkbox · the kind's glyph or the image · number and name (two lines before it clips, REG-355) · "qty unit × unit price" left and the total right · a one-line description · the tags (Section · Approved / Declined · Optional · the Print-as word) · the kebab; a section is a tinted card with its count and its fold, its lines indented under it; a "View by" bucket is a caption row with its sum. A tap opens a **bottom sheet** with the line's fields — Item name · Description (the Expand dialog) · Qty · Unit · Cost · Markup · Unit price · Discount · GST · Kind · Room · Category · Section label · Add an image — each the DS field, saved on leaving it through the same patch the grid uses, **Done** to close; the kebab is the row menu as a sheet (REG-417). The Add Item row sits under the cards, its field on its own line. The desktop and the tablet keep the grid; the mock wins — the owner may redraw the card and the sheet, and the build follows. | user-directed · invented layout |
| REG-598 | Company | **A gallery card's menu measures its side** (found by the Price book probe, 2026-09-24). The libraries' cards (REG-538) opened their kebab menu downward whatever the card's place, so a card in the last row near the screen's foot drew its menu past the viewport — the bottom rows (Delete) unreachable. The card's press now asks `menuSideFor` (REG-286's one rule) with the menu's row count, as the list rows already did. | bug fix |
| REG-599 | Settings · Leads | **Lead sources the studio edits** (Tech sheet row 62, Flipstone — "add new dropdowns to lead sources, add TikTok etc.", live in 1.0; the Leads round's L1, owner 2026-09-24: no mocks, build on the DS). Settings › Leads › **Lead sources** under Stage movement: the trades card's chips (REG-561) with a lead count each; Add a source; Retire on a source with leads (its leads keep it, the pickers stop offering it — a dashed, struck chip; Restore brings it back); Remove on one with none; Others stays last. Settings key `lead_sources`; the defaults are the enum plus **TikTok**. The lead form's Lead source, the import mapping, the Filters modal's Sources and the listing read the list (`fetchLeadSources` · `liveSourceNames`): the form offers the live ones plus the lead's own retired one; the filter offers the live ones plus any retired source still on a lead; an import maps old exports by every name, retired included. Qanvast stays the enum's special case (Return Lead). | user-directed |
| REG-600 | Leads · Lead Details | **Last contact at a glance** (Tech sheet row 88, Cove — "follow-up visibility: timestamps on uploaded images, viewable at a glance", live in 1.0; the Leads round's L2). Every lead row carries its **last contact** — the latest of the Notes tab's contact logs and the Contacted stage's entries (their typed "when", else the move's time) with its channel and outcome, derived in the one row builder (`lastContactOf`, no new table). The listing gains a **Last contact** column (off until switched on in Column settings; sortable; "12 Sep · 14:32" over "WhatsApp · Follow up"; "No contact yet"); the Overview's Lead stages card opens with a Last contact line that leads to the Notes tab; the Notes tab's attachment tiles print their upload time under the name (the tile grows 158 → 174, a deviation from 931:45548). The Move stage window's file tiles carry names alone (a simulated upload) and are unchanged. | user-directed |
| REG-601 | Company · Leads | **Managers, and who sees which leads** (Tech sheet row 25, Dyel — the owner assigns, a manager below her assigns to designers, designers cannot reassign, the manager sees only what he was given; the Leads round's L6, owner 2026-09-24: build on the DS). **Company › Members** goes live: one row per member (avatar · name · email · Role · Reports to · leads held) on Profile's card shell; roles are Owner · Admin · Manager · Designer (`ROLES`), a Designer picks the Manager they report to (`members.manager_id`, migration 0031); Owners and Admins edit, the last Owner cannot be demoted. **Settings › Leads › Who sees leads**: Everyone (today) or Only the leads assigned to them (`lead_visibility`) — under it a Manager sees their own and their team's leads (the team-view answer to the open question: a manager who reassigns must still follow the lead), a Designer their own, Owners and Admins everything; the listing, the kanban, the Tasks rail and a lead's own pages follow (`lib/leads/visibility.ts`; a lead outside the viewer's sight is not found). **Assignment follows the role whatever the visibility**: Owners and Admins assign anyone; a Manager's Assign popover, lead form and New step offer their team and themselves (other rows show "outside your team", locked); a Designer's popover is read-only with "Designers keep the leads assigned to them. Ask your manager to reassign." (`lib/leads/assign-policy.ts`, checked again by `assignMembers`). Demo: James is the Manager, Sarah and Michelle his team (repair R39). | user-directed |
| REG-602 | Leads · Settings · Company | **Lead capture: the website's email and Meta Lead Ads** (Tech sheet rows 9 · 17, Dyel — "website leads appear in the OM leads dashboard", Cove — "Meta Ads lead integration"; the Leads round's L5, owner 2026-09-24: build on the DS). **Settings › Leads › Lead capture**: the studio's intake address (`leads+{studio}@in.openmaison.sg` — the domain is invented) with Copy, the arrivals so far, the Meta state, and a "Simulate an enquiry arriving (demo)" link. **Company › Integrations** goes live on Profile's card shell: Qanvast (connected — the return flow), Website form by email (the address again), **Meta Lead Ads** (Connect a Facebook page → a stand-in page, the lead forms with switches, the field map, Simulate a form submission), and Google Calendar · Xero · Zoho Books · Zapier as Coming. An arrival is recorded the way the platform would record it — a one-row import (`captureLead`, `imports.file_name` "Website form · {studio}" / "Meta Lead Ads · {form}") that lands in the listing with the source Website or Meta Ads and the primary band; a known phone goes to Review imported leads. The Add Lead menu's dormant "Link to Email" and "Connect to Ads" now open the two cards; Zapier still answers coming soon. The simulated enquiries are invented demo names. Settings key `lead_capture`. | user-directed · invented copy |
| REG-603 | Leads · Imports | **Import an XLSX or a lead-sheet PDF** (Tech sheet row 101, Dyel — "a way to upload PDF (Homematch)", live in 1.0; the Leads round's L3). The import wizard's step 1 takes CSV, **XLSX** (`lib/xlsx.ts`, a dependency-free reader of the first worksheet) and **PDF** (`lib/pdf-text.ts` on pdf.js, loaded only when a PDF is dropped, the worker served from `/pdfjs`; `lib/leads/homematch.ts` reads a lead sheet as a table with a name and a phone column, or as labelled "Name: … Phone: …" blocks — anything else is refused with "Couldn't find leads in this PDF"). From step 2 on it is the wizard as mocked — mapping, routing, duplicates, the review sheet. An .xls is asked to be saved as XLSX or CSV. **Tuned to the real Homematch sample when it arrives** — the sheet's two shapes are the reader's assumption. | user-directed |
| REG-604 | Cross · Settings | **Notifications: the feed, the rules, the messages** (Tech sheet rows 68 · 71 · 72 · 73, Flipstone · Cove — email sequences, the 30-minute follow-up, closed leads stop, WhatsApp, live in 1.0; rows 8 · 10 · 28, Dyel — WhatsApp on a new lead, every message's copy with a dynamic link for every recipient, shareable links everywhere; the Leads round's L4, owner 2026-09-24: build on the DS). **The bell** (the rail's item and the phone bar's) opens a Drawer: what happened — a lead arrived (Owners and Admins), a lead was assigned (the assignee), a quote needs approval (the approvers incl. the backup), a client confirmed or signed (the lead's members and Owners) — as stored rows (`notifications`, migration 0032, raised by the actions through `lib/notifications/emit.ts`; the actor never hears their own act), woven with the **follow-ups due now**, derived and never stored: a New lead untouched past the delay, an overdue task. A row opens its record and is read; "also by WhatsApp" names the channels the rules sent it on (the sending is the platform's); Mark all as read; the unread count on the rail. **Settings › Notifications** (a fourth live module): Events × channels (in app · email · WhatsApp switches per event; who receives each is the event's), Follow-ups (the untouched delay 15 min … 1 day, "No follow-ups once a lead is Won, Lost or On Hold"), and **Messages** — every email and WhatsApp with its fields (`{lead_name}` `{studio}` `{member}` `{actor}` `{source}` `{quote_number}` `{link}`) previewed on a sample lead, Edit with the field chips and Reset to the house draft. Defaults: in-app for all; WhatsApp for a new lead, an untouched lead, a confirmation and a signature; email for approvals and assignments. The copy of every message is invented and reads as a proposal. Repair R40 seeds the demo's rows. | user-directed · invented copy |

Source = `qanvast-id-tool` at `67f42e469`, read whole by five readers (inventories banked under `design-source/context/parity/leads-1.0-inventory/`), plus the live app on :3001 (Staging, demo studio Design Loft SG) browsed read-only. (`countLiveSigningLinks`: contracts Sent to Client and unsigned bundles); Delete, Delete Forever, Empty Trash and the Leads settings are Owners' and Admins' (`assertCanDelete`, the menus drop the item, Settings › Leads reads "Only Owners and Admins change these" for anyone else — reachable only by changing Dane's role, REG-601). | REG-608 | Leads · Notifications · Parity round 2 | **Follow-ups and notifications from 1.0** (the parity programme's round 2 — Q-03 · Q-06 · Q-23 of the owner's answers, 2026-09-24; 1.0's behaviour on 2.0's components). **A follow-up is a task** (Q-03): no `next_follow_up_at` column — a lead's next follow-up is its earliest dated open task, and `lead_tasks.kind` gains **Follow-up** (`TASK_KINDS`). The Contacted reminder (the Move stage window's Suggested tasks and the Notes tab's Add tasks) is created as a dated Follow-up for the lead's members, and so is the task a quote's due date raises. The LEAD STAGES card takes a **Next follow-up** line under Last contact (date · time · task; "Overdue · " in red; "None set — set a follow-up" when there is none) that opens the task, or the **Set a follow-up** dialog (When · Task prefilled "Follow up with {name}"; `setFollowUp` creates the Follow-up for the lead's members, else for you); the listing takes a hidden-by-default **Next follow-up** column (date over task, sortable, undated last) and the Filters modal a **Quick filters** pane — Overdue follow-ups · Unassigned · Unseen, AND-ed as 1.0's, without Hot (Q-10) or Needs attention (no 2.0 meaning; L-22 done here, not in round 4). **Notifications** (Q-23 · Q-06 — rows, rules and messages on Settings › Notifications): *A lead's stage changes* (the window, the pill and Return Lead alike — to Owners, Admins and the lead's members, never the actor; body "On hold · {reason}" / "Lost · {reason}"; Undo deletes the rows its move raised), *A task is assigned to you* (created with you on it, added later, a template, a quote's due date), *A task is due in 30 minutes* (derived from the open tasks — "Follow-up in n min: …" / "Due in n min: …", 1.0's T−30 reminder), *An On Hold date is reached* (derived from the On Hold log's `followUpAt`: "Hold ended — revisit {name}"), *A client answers the questionnaire* (rules and messages only — 2.0 has no client-side answer yet; it fires once the portal's answer lands). Q-29 and Q-32 were "no": tasks stay open on Lost and keep their assignees. **(6) settings lists of switches** — the question bank and the custom fields rows are the Notifications module's event table (a bordered list under a caption head, the switches centred under their column names, order · edit · delete at the row's end; they carried per-row "Required / Enabled" labels and stacked arrows); **(7)** Members' preferences line is one line per row (switch · summary, ellipsized · Preferences); **(8)** the Return wizard's *Rules* is the DS Link. Probes: r2 and r5 assertions follow the new rows / chips; all seven parity probes re-run on fresh test copies. Screenshots `design-source/verify/audit-2026-09-24/`. Rule (the checklist): a new surface reuses the closest built pattern — `Chips`, `StatTile`, the event table, the card's own row, the listing's table model — never a page-local twin. | user-directed · bug fix |

| REG-623 | Quotes · Company · Parity (quote editor) round 1 | **Lines and money from 1.0** (the calls of REG-622, "As recommended"). **Q-07** *By client* joins Print as (`PRINT_AS` `client`, 1.0's `by_client` price type): the word prints in place of the amount, nothing counts, the studio bears no cost (FOC · Inclusive · Packaged still meet the margin). **Q-08** the catalogue's **Vendor/Sub** column is drawn: `quote_items.vendor` (≤ 80, migration `0038_quote_item_vendor_link`, additive); a line picked from My Items takes the item's vendor, Save to Your Library carries it back, saved sections keep it; the cell is the house label picker — My Items' vendors first, then the ones added on the quote (`ItemLabels.vendors`, *Add vendor* through the name dialog) and the ones in use; drag-fill, ⌘D and Set value reach it; the phone's line sheet has it; never printed. **Q-09** the **Website Link** column is drawn: `quote_items.link` (http / https only — a bare address takes https://, anything else is refused with a snackbar and the stored link stays; `cleanLink` · `shortLink`); at rest the short address in the primary ink with an Open button (new tab), the whole link in the box; never printed. Both columns travel through paste (headers *Vendor · Supplier · Subcontractor* and *Website · Link · URL*; the price book's import maps a Vendor column per row, Website Link is left out there), the Items export (two columns before Total), Duplicate, Revise and undo. **Q-13** a line that sells below its cost (its total after the line discount, before GST, under Qty × Cost; never a word line, a zero total or a declined line — 1.0's B-48) wears a red *Below cost* tag after its name with the figures in its tooltip, only while the Cost column is on (a screen shown with "Show only what the client sees" never says it); the phone's card too. **Q-14** By category gains *Share · Margin* chips for Owners (the house `Chips`): each category's margin on its own lines before the quote's discounts — rebates counted, FOC / Inclusive / Packaged cost included — with 1.0's bands on the bar (≥ 20 % green, 10–20 yellow, under 10 red), a loss in red, "n · no price" for a category with nothing selling (`categoryShares` `costMinor` · `marginPct`, `trueCostMinor`). **§8.2 gap 1 (E-32)** undo / redo wrote the lines back without *Print as one line* and *Rebate* — `replaceQuoteItems` now carries both, and the vendor and link. **Bug fixes found on the way:** the Total cell of a Print-as line lost the start of "S$1,950.00 · not counted" under the frozen edge (100 px of text room) — the figure now sits struck through under the word, "not counted" in the cell's tooltip, *On behalf* keeps "no GST", a long sub-total label ends in "…"; the phone's line sheet had no side gutter and cut its first field's label (REG-372's 16 px now, 12 on top). Demo data: repair **R46** gives the seven demo lines picked from My Items their item's vendor (+ two checks); `fixtures/baseline.json` = the committed baseline plus those seven vendors (the day's other local edits stay out of it). Invented copy: *Add vendor* · *Add link* · *Open the link* · *Not a web address — a link starts with https://* · *A product or spec page — never printed* · *Below cost* · *Sells for {S$}, costs {S$} — {S$} under its cost* · *Share* · *Margin* · *margin before the quote’s discounts* · *n · no price* · *Prints “{word}” — {S$} not counted* · *no GST* · *The client supplies it — prints “By client”, not counted*. | REG-624 | Quotes · Settings · Notifications · Parity (quote editor) round 2 | **Approval and the banner** (the calls of REG-622, "As recommended"). **Q-22** approval on the record — `quotes.submitted_by · submitted_at · approved_by · review_note · reviewed_by · reviewed_at · client_sent_at` (migration `0039_quote_approval_record`, additive): a quote waiting at Sent for Approval shows the **approval bar** under the tabs (the editor's banner card, template approval's words): "{Name} sent this quote for approval {ago}." — an approver (Owner · Admin · the backup approver) gets **Request changes · Approve**, anyone else "Waiting for an Owner, an Admin or the backup approver". *Approve* (`approveQuote`) → Approved, on the record as theirs; *Request changes* (`requestQuoteChanges`) asks for a note (the template's `NoteDialog`, now `required`) and sends the quote back to Draft with it; the preparer then reads "Changes requested by {Name} {ago} — {note} Send it again when it's ready." at the top until the next send (dismissable for the visit); the note stays on the record. Two new notification events, both to whoever sent it: *Your quote is approved* (`quote_approved`) and *Changes are asked on your quote* (`quote_changes`, the note as the body), with email and WhatsApp copies in Settings › Notifications. **Q-23** an approver's own Send approves at once: at Draft their Send is the client's menu (Send for confirmation · Send for signature), and `sendQuote` / `sendQuoteForConfirmation` take it straight to Sent to Client, approved by them. **Q-04** no hard lock — a quote the client has shows the **sent banner** with *Revise — new version*: "Sent to the client {ago} — they have version n. Edits here change what they see on their link; to change the quote and keep what they saw, revise it." · "{client} confirmed version n {ago}. To change it, revise — this version stays as they confirmed it." · "Signed — version n is the agreed quote. To change it, revise — this version stays as signed." **§8.2 gaps:** (2) the lead's Quotes tab and Tools › Quotes show Approved "approvers only" and disabled for anyone else, as the editor's pill does (`QuotesTable canApprove`); (3) Settings › Quotes is the Owners' and Admins' — the page says so and guards every control ("Only Owners and Admins change the quote settings"; trades' add / remove hidden), and **every Settings setter** (Leads' too) now checks the role on the server (`assertRunsStudio`); (4) Revise and Duplicate start clean — the client's confirmation, the approval record and its notes stay with the kept version (Duplicate also restarts at version 1); (5) the Activity section and the lead's Activity read each send by its own stamp (sent for approval by · changes asked by · approved by · sent to the client · confirmed by), no longer by the quote's current status. Demo data: repair **R47** gives the six demo quotes that passed approval their sender (the creator), their approver (the Owner) and — the three signed ones — the day they went to the client (+ four checks); the baseline is rebuilt as the committed baseline + the repair rules (never the day's local edits). | REG-631 | Contracts · Parity round 1 | **The record holds** (contracts parity Q-01…Q-07; `07-contracts-1.0-to-2.0-audit-2026-09-25.md` §7 round 1). **Q-01 · Q-02 · Q-03** keep 2.0's contract record, "the client signs the contract and the quote is signed through it", and the three statuses (nothing to build). **Q-05** the signed copy: every path that signs a contract — online, in a bundle, Mark as signed, a list's Signed, an already-signed import — keeps its text with each chip's value in place (Today's date = the signing day), the letterhead it was signed under, the studio signature it printed and a fingerprint (1.0's per-document hash, B-26); Preview, the signing page, the bundle page, every print and the editor itself show that copy, never today's records (`lib/contracts/snapshot.ts` pure · `signedSnapshot` in `lib/contracts/queries.ts` · `ContractPaper letterhead`); the editor shows a Signed contract as its kept copy where the paper was. **Q-06** the signing page holds the fingerprint of the text it shows; a document changed meanwhile is refused and the page reloads the new text (*This agreement was updated while you were reading — please read it again before signing*); a bundle checks the whole set's fingerprint (1.0's whole-set hash, B-29). **Q-04** at Signed the title, recipient and contract date lock with the paper (server refuses too); one move-back rule for the editor's pill and every list's status cell (`lib/contracts/status.ts` `applyContractStatus`, `lib/contracts/policy.ts` `unsignBlock`): a signature the client made online stays signed (*Signed by the client online — a signed contract stays signed. Duplicate it to change the terms.*), a studio-marked one moves back only for an Owner or an Admin, after the confirm *Move back to {status}?*, and lets go of every signing stamp (date, signer, NRIC, signed copy, the kept copy); the quote it signed stays Signed. The editor's banner (the quote's sent banner, REG-624): out for signature, and what an edit does to the client's open page; signed online / marked signed. **Q-07** a signing link signs only a contract at Sent to Client (or a live bundle): a Draft (or one moved back) shows *Not ready yet*, an archived or deleted contract, or one whose lead was deleted, shows *This link is no longer active* (1.0's words, B-73) — neither carries the document; copying a Draft's link (the header, Preview, the lists, the Send dialog's Copy link) sends it (*Signing link copied — the contract is Sent to Client now*; a blank document is not shared). **§8.2 gaps fixed:** 1 (the lists' un-sign kept the stamps) · 2 (a signed contract's meta still changed) · 3 (chips never froze) · 4 (the link signed Drafts, archived contracts, deleted leads' — `signContractOnline` / `signBundleOnline` refuse) · 5 (the lists' delete said "cannot be undone"; both now *You can restore it from Deleted Contracts.*) · 6 (Preview printed the signed-in member's signature and no NRIC — now the signing page's rule: the creator's) · 7 (the NRIC left the page whole — the page sends only the masked form; un-signing clears it) · 8 (the server kept `<iframe>`, `<style>`, single-quoted handlers, and cut a document with a picture mid-tag — `lib/contracts/sanitize.ts`, the editor's own markup only, on save and at print; a document over 4,000,000 characters is refused whole) · 9 (a phone kept its first eight digits, "+65 9123 4567" → 65912345 — now `phoneDigits`, the last eight). Migration `0042_contract_signed_copy` (`contracts.signed_body · signed_studio_signature · signed_letterhead · document_hash`); repair **R50** (the demo's three signed contracts keep their copy as of their signing day; stamps on unsigned contracts cleared; cut phones put back) + three checks; baseline rebuilt. Invented copy: the banner's two sentences, *Move back to {status}?* and its message, *Not ready yet* and its line, *This link is no longer active* as a title, the refusal snackbar, *Signing link copied — the contract is Sent to Client now*, *Add some content before sharing the link*, *The document is too large to save — use a smaller picture*, *A signed contract stays as it was signed*, the two move-back rules. Found on the way (UI sweep backlog): U-05 the Quotes tab's delete wording, U-06 the quote's Copy link, U-07 no archived view. Probe `design-source/verify/contracts-parity/verify-ct-r1.cjs` (35/35); shots `r1-*.png`. | user-directed |
| REG-632 | Contracts · Parity round 2 | **The quote and the issuing entity** (contracts parity Q-08…Q-14). **Q-08** a contract names its quote from the start — the one it was raised from, else the lead's Signed quote, else its latest — stored, and read by that id only (`pickQuote` in `lib/contracts/snapshot.ts`; `defaultQuote`); the meta row's **Quote** field (the recipient chip's twin: *OM-… · S$…* ▾, the lead's quotes with total and status, *No quote*) changes it; a named quote later archived or deleted keeps being read and the editor says so (*{number} was {archived / deleted}. the meta row's **Issued by** (offered when Company › Profile names two or more entities) changes it until signing; the letterhead, the Studio name · address · UEN chips and the WhatsApp draft follow the entity's own name, logo, address and UEN, the address falling back to the studio's and a registration number never inheriting (`lib/contracts/issuer.ts` `contractIssuer`, the quote's `docLetterhead` rule; the signed copy keeps the letterhead it was signed under). **Q-11** a *GST reg. no.* chip (the issuer's GST number; blank when the entity is not GST-registered); no *Start date* (2.0's key collection is a period). **Q-12** Smart Text's groups say how many records are empty (*n empty*) and an empty record reads *Empty* (it prints its label in brackets). **Q-13** a default contract template: *Set as default* in the gallery's kebab, a *Default* chip on its card and in the picker, which opens with it picked; the Overview's and the Won step's Create Contract (`?new=1`) start from it (`contract_templates.is_default`, `setDefaultContractTemplate`, `createContractDoc templateId 'default'`). **Q-14** the Won step's *Upload* (a contract signed on paper) becomes a Signed uploaded contract on the lead, named by its file, its copy kept, reading and signing through the quote the step names — once per file, from the move and from an edit of the step (`lib/contracts/won.ts` `recordWonUpload`). **§8.2 gaps fixed:** 10 (the raised-from quote replaced without a word) · 11 (the issuing entity never written) · 12 (the quote ↔ contract link invisible; the quote printed the lead as its signer) · 13 (the editor loaded the lead's quotes and showed none — they are the Quote field's choices). Migration `0043_contract_template_default`; repair **R51** (a contract naming no quote takes the one it was reading; it takes that quote's issuer; the demo's Won logs name their lead's signed contract instead of the step's placeholder file — REG-343) + three checks; baseline rebuilt. | REG-633 | Contracts · Parity round 3 | **Signing** (contracts parity Q-15…Q-20). **Q-15** 1.0's per-document gates (the signing library's `requires_scroll · requires_accept · accept_label`, B-02): eSignatures › *Before the client signs* — *Read to the end* (they reach the last line before they can go on; not for an uploaded PDF) and *Tick to accept* with *The words they tick* (default *I have read and agree to this document.*) — on a contract and on a template; new contracts and templates start with both on, a template's gates travel into the contracts made from it, existing ones keep none; the signing page waits for both before *Send code* (*Read to the end of the agreement to continue* · *You've read to the end*), and the server refuses a signature whose words were never ticked. **Q-16** a bundle is read one document per screen in the order the studio ticked them (*Document i of n · title* over a progress bar; *Continue* · *Continue to sign*), each with its own gates; one signature — typed or drawn — covers them all; the agreement sentence names what is signed (*I, {name}, agree to the {title} and quotation {number} from {issuer}, as set out above. I understand that this is my digital agreement to proceed.*; a bundle names every document); Send together numbers the documents as ticked and keeps that order (`contracts.bundle_position`). **Q-17** the client's first open of a link out for signature is kept (`viewed_at` on the contract and the bundle): the lists' Sent cell reads *Viewed {date}*, the editor's banner *opened … / not opened yet*, Activity *{client} opened the contract "…"*. **Q-18** the client types or draws (*How you sign* — Type · Draw chips; the member's Signature pad, now the shared `components/om/signature-pad.tsx`); a drawn signature prints on the paper. **Q-19** the signing record (1.0's certificate in 2.0's words, `components/contracts/sign/signing-record.tsx`): signed by, typed or drawn, the NRIC, *A one-time code sent to +65 •••• 4567*, the device and time zone, link opened, accepted, signed, the documents (a bundle), the fingerprint (and *Marked signed by the studio on …* for a studio-marked one) — under the paper on the client's signed page and in Preview, printed with *Download a copy*, and behind the editor banner's *Signing record* (`signature_kind · signature_data · signer_phone · signer_user_agent · signer_timezone · verification_method · accepted_at`; the bundle's `signing_hash` = 1.0's whole-set hash). **Q-20** the signing page names the quote it signs (*It signs quotation {number} — view the quote*), which opens read-only at `/sign/{id}/quote` (the confirmation page's document, no card; only while the link is open or signed). **§8.2 gap 14 fixed** (the bundle's screens disagreed): one link per bundled document everywhere (`contractLink`), the *Sent together* chip in the editor, a document sent on its own leaves its bundle, the bundle page's order is the ticked one, a document signed on its own shows as signed and asks nothing, Send together is addressed to the documents' recipient, its Copy link records the bundle so the copied link opens (*Signing link copied — the documents are Sent to Client now*), the id's fallback is hex. Found and fixed on the way: the server hashed a bundle's documents in another order than the page, so every bundle signature was refused. Migration `0044_contract_signing_record`; repair **R52** (an online signature from before keeps a typed kind and the phone code; old bundled documents take their creation order) + three checks; baseline rebuilt. | REG-634 | Contracts · Parity round 4 | **Lists, roles and follow-through** (contracts parity Q-21…Q-25 + §8.2 gaps 15–25). **Q-21** who may: delete, restore, delete forever and empty the trash — the contract's creator, an Owner or an Admin (the quote's rule, REG-625; | REG-638 | Quote X-Ray · Parity round 1 | **The reading tells the truth** (X-Ray parity Q-01…Q-09; `09-quote-x-ray-1.0-to-2.0-audit-2026-09-25.md` §7 round 1). **Engine 2** (`lib/xray/compare.ts`, `ENGINE = 2`, pure). **Q-01** one comparison kind, 1.0's retired objectives left behind (nothing to remove). **Q-02** *Yours* is what the quote counts — `lib/xray/lines.ts` `yourLinesFrom` (shared by the loader and the repair): items in the quote's order (`flattenItems`), optional lines, declined lines and a declined section's lines out; a line printing a word (FOC · Inclusive · KIV · By client · Packaged · custom) stays as covered with its word and no price (`yourWord`, shown in the Yours cell); an untitled empty line is skipped. The summary's footnote now says "Both sides are compared on their line totals, before discounts and GST — with them, OM-10008 comes to S$68,176.23" (`quoteTotalMinor` kept at the reading). **Q-03** matching: stems (plural), a short list of trade words that mean the same work (`SAME`: hack · demolish · dismantle · removal; cabinetry; worktop → countertop; socket → point …), two-letter work words (TV · WC · DB · 3D), a name-only score beside name + description, the trade and the room setting how many shared words a pair needs (same trade + same room 0.34 · open room 0.4 · clash 0.5; across trades 0.5 / 0.6 / 0.8), "taking out is not putting in" (a hacking line never pairs with a laying line — 1.0's false merge B-29), one shared item noun in the same trade and room (a quartz and a solid-surface countertop), one line against several (a leftover line joins a pair of its trade in a room that fits), and a trade lump sum ("Plumbing works, kitchen and bathrooms", 1 lot) against the other side's lone lines of that trade. Rows carry `yourIds` · `theirIdx`. The seeded OM-10008 reading no longer tells the client Flo Design leaves out the kitchen cabinets it lists (E-98). **Q-04** rates, not lump sums, when one line each side gives a quantity in the same unit (metres stay metres — 1.0 read them as feet); both quantities print under the figures (`qty`). **Q-05** 1.0's fact pool: missing (their quote leaves it out) · cheaper (*Same item, different price* — ours lower by ≥ 2 %) · below (*Priced well below ours* — under 60 % of our rate or total) · unpriced · material (1.0's sixteen, `lib/xray/materials.ts`, carried word for word with two fixes: a worktop needs a worktop word, and the glass-house test reads the same use in one of your lines) · brand (your named brand where theirs names none; their brand with an impact, `BrandNote.ask`), weighed as 1.0 weighs them, the three heaviest said, one per row, a lone question-only point dropped. **Q-06** the label *Priced well below ours* (2.0 holds no market rates). **Q-07** Say this and the draft say "the other quote", never the studio (the evidence, for the studio, keeps the name); names keep their capitals and lose their "Supply and install" (`lowerFirst` · `plainName` · `short` at a word). **Q-08** `draftMessage(result, questions)` builds the draft from the screen — a point switched to its question goes out as the question — with 1.0's like-for-like line before the sign-off, and a reading with no point sends that line alone. **Q-09** key points: a brand both quotes name is left out; one only theirs names with nothing to raise joins "We also checked … in their quote — nothing to flag." (tone `checked`); your advantage says what their quote names in that trade; sources print as "Sources · renotalk.com · …" (no link colour, no open glyph); Upload two quotes keeps your file's brand column (`refAsYours`). One sign: the table's *Difference* is yours − theirs, as the *Real difference* tile ("−" green = ours less); money in the house formats (`money2`, `moneyCompact`); `StatTile` for the three tiles; no signed zero. The intro's promise now says "missing items, prices worth a question, materials and brands". A reading keeps both sides' lines (`quote_comparisons.inputs_json`), its engine (`engine`) and an upload's own file (`your_file`); index on `lead_id` — migration `0046_xray_reading` (additive). Repair: R33 reads through `yourLinesFrom` and keeps inputs + engine; **R54** re-reads every engine-1 reading (same quotes, date and maker) and removes a file compared with itself (E-112); two checks. Probe `design-source/verify/x-ray-parity/verify-xr-r1.cjs` 27/27. | user-directed |
| REG-639 | Quote X-Ray · Quotes · Parity round 2 | **Reading the files, finding the way** (X-Ray parity Q-10…Q-13 + §8.2 gaps 14–24). **Q-10** a file is read the moment it is picked (`lib/xray/reading.ts` `readSide`, in the browser): a spreadsheet (XLSX · CSV) for real through Upload a quote's reader (`readRows`, REG-627) — its lines, trades, rooms, quantities and amounts — a PDF or a photo as one of the three reference quotes by name (the platform reads the rest); an unreadable file, a type outside PDF · image · CSV · Excel, or a file over 10 MB is said at once under its dropzone; a readable one shows "28 lines read from Flo Design's quote · S$31,140.00" under a file row (its type's glyph, no stock thumbnail — E-109); a sheet asks for *Their studio's name (optional)* (else "The other studio"). The run takes a sheet's lines from the browser and checks them again on the server (`cleanLines`: ≤ 500 lines, names ≤ 200, amounts ≥ 0). Reference names are read by whole words (`readOtherQuote`: "floor plan.pdf", "workflow.xlsx", "information.pdf", "scope of works.pdf" no longer read as Flo or Cove — E-95; Upload a quote shares the fix). **Q-11** the house select finds as you type (type-ahead in `components/om/text-field.tsx`'s list — every select gains it); the lead list picks by id (two leads with one label read "… (2)"); a lead with one quote has it chosen (1.0's chip); a quote with no lines to compare can't be chosen ("No lines to compare yet"; `XrayQuote.lineCount`); a lead with no quote says so and offers *Create a quote* (the Create Quote window) and *Upload your quote instead*, which keeps the lead in Upload two quotes (a locked lead box, clearable off a lead's page); a lead's page keeps its lead in Upload mode too (E-86); a `?quote=` that is not one of the lead's live quotes is not chosen (E-17). **Q-12** the record's crumbs name its lead and the lead's X-Ray page (E-84); Back, New comparison, the Recent rows and "All comparisons for {lead}" keep the lead's scope (E-85); a run returns to the page it started from; the lead's Activity row returns to Activity; the quick link returns to the tab it was pressed on; the quote editor's kebab keeps the editor's own origin; *Compare with another quote* joins the row menus of the lead's Quotes tab and Tools › Quotes (a phone's way into X-Ray from a lead, E-81). The Quotes table's row menu now opens through the body portal (`PortalMenu`) — an inline menu was clipped by the card's sideways scroller on a one-row table. **Q-13** Recent lists every comparison, newest first, with a search ("Search by lead, quote or studio") once it passes eight; a lead's page lists all of that lead's (E-87). House pieces: X-Ray's own ghosts (`components/xray/xray-skeletons.tsx` — setup and record; E-105), the page ends clear of the FAB and the dock (`--fab-clearance`, E-107), Column settings takes three pins (E-106), the phone card labels the other side's figure with its studio (E-113). Probe `design-source/verify/x-ray-parity/verify-xr-r2.cjs` 31/31; regression r1 · qe-r3 · ct-r2 · ct-r4 green. | user-directed |

its menu through `PortalMenu`) offers *Not the same item* on a pair and *Same item as…* on a lone line (the house `PickOneDialog`, listing the other side's lone lines); the reading is read again over the lines it kept (`correctComparison` · `reread`), so the rows, figures, headline, points, key points and draft follow; corrections carried where both lines still exist — `rerunComparison`). **Q-16** Copy and Open WhatsApp stamp the reading with the text as it went (`recordComparisonSent` — `sent_at · sent_by · sent_text · sent_via`); WhatsApp opens the lead's chat only when the lead's number is a valid SG number (`isSgPhone`), else WhatsApp's picker. **Q-17** the record names its quote as a link to the editor (with the way back) or says it was deleted, names who ran it ("for Dane"), and its kebab offers Run again · Open the quote · Delete (the house confirm;
